and hence, from (
7.40
), we have

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(1)}_{1,2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{T_{i}^{1}\in{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}_{1,2}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{1}_{i}\in[0,1]}\,\,1\Big]{}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{1}_{i}\in[0,1]}\,\,\mathbbmss{1}_{\exists(T^{2}_{j},H^{2}_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{2}_{j}\in D_{r}(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i},H^{2}_{j})}\mathbbmss{1}_{A^{(1)}_{1,2}}\Big]{}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{1}_{i}\in[0,1]}\,\,\sum_{(T^{2}_{j},H^{2}_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{2}_{j}\in D_{r}(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i},H^{2}_{j})}\mathbbmss{1}_{A^{(1)}_{1,2}}\Big]{}

(7.41)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq:h1vb2}),(\ref{eq:h1vb1})}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{1}_{i}\in[0,1]}\,\,\sum_{(T^{2}_{j},H^{2}_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{2}_{j}\in D_{\ell}(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i},H^{2}_{j})}\mathbbmss{1}_{A^{(2)}_{2,1}}\Big]=\Lambda^{(2)}_{2,1}.

We can prove the other equality
\Lambda^{(2)}_{1,2}=\Lambda^{(1)}_{2,1}
similarly.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.20
part (
iii
).

Let us now compute the contribution of mixed handovers by evaluating
\Lambda^{(1)}_{2,1}
and
\Lambda^{(2)}_{2,1}
. By applying the multivariate Campbell Mecke formula to the factorial power of order 2 of
{\mathcal{H}}
in (
7.36
), similarly to what was done for Equation (
4.21
) in Theorem
4.26
for the single-speed case, we obtain that, for
k=1,2
,

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\Lambda^{(k)}_{2,1}

\displaystyle=4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\int_{D_{\ell}(t_{1},h_{1},h_{2})}\!\!\!\mathbb{E}^{(t_{1},h_{1}),(t_{2},h_{2})}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{A^{(k)}_{2,1}}\left({\mathcal{H}}^{1}+{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\right)\right]{\rm d}t_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{1}\,{\rm d}t_{1}{}

(7.42)

\displaystyle=4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\int_{D_{\ell}(t_{1},h_{1},h_{2})}\!\!\!\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{A^{(k)}_{2,1}}\left({\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(t_{1},h_{1})}+{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(t_{2},h_{2})}\right)\right]{\rm d}t_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{1}\,{\rm d}t_{1},

where the probability measure is

(7.43)

\mathbb{P}^{(t_{1},h_{1}),(t_{2},h_{2})}_{{\mathcal{H}}}:=\mathbb{P}^{(t_{1},h_{1})}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{1}}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{(t_{2},h_{2})}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{2}},

namely the two-point Palm probability measure on
{\mathcal{H}}
with the first point belonging to
{\mathcal{H}}^{1}
and the second point belonging to
{\mathcal{H}}^{2}
. Separating the integral (
7.42
) into the cases,
h_{2}\in[0,h_{1}]
, for which
D_{\ell}(t_{1},h_{1},h_{2})=(-\infty,t_{1}-t^{*}]
, and
h_{2}\in(h_{1},\infty)
, for which
D_{\ell}=(-\infty,t_{1}]
, from Lemma
7.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\!\!\!\Lambda^{(k)}_{2,1}

\displaystyle=4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}\!\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{h_{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{t_{1}-t^{*}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{A^{(k)}_{2,1}}\left({\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(t_{1},h_{1})}+{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(t_{2},h_{2})}\right)\right]\,{\rm d}t_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{1}\,{\rm d}t_{1}{}

(7.44)

\displaystyle\quad+\;4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}\!\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{t_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{A^{(k)}_{2,1}}\left({\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(t_{1},h_{1})}+{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(t_{2},h_{2})}\right)\right]\,{\rm d}t_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{1}\,{\rm d}t_{1},

where
t^{*}=\frac{1}{v_{1}v_{2}}(h_{1}^{2}-h_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
as in (
7.15
).
Using the intersection points
(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{h}_{1}),(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
of the radial birds, determined in (
7.12
) and (
7.22
), we can redefine the event
A^{(k)}_{2,1}
, from (
7.1.8
), for
k=1,2
, as

(7.45)

A^{(k)}_{2,1}:=\{(\hat{s}_{k},\hat{h}_{k})\text{ is a handover point}\}.

Under the two-point Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{(t_{1},h_{1}),(t_{2},h_{2})}_{{\mathcal{H}}}
,
the required probability of the events
A^{(k)}_{2,1}
, for
k=1,2
,
turns out to be a function of
t_{1}-t_{2}
, given
h_{1},h_{2}
.
This will be clear later in (
7.51
) in the proof of the following lemma.

Lemma 7.21
.

Suppose
h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
t_{2}\leq t_{1}
, for
h_{1}<h_{2}
and
t_{2}\leq t_{1}-t^{*}
, for
h_{1}>h_{2}
, where
t^{*}
is defined in (
7.15
). Under the two-point Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{(t_{1},h_{1}),(t_{2},h_{2})}_{{\mathcal{H}}}
, the probability of the event
A^{(k)}_{2,1}
, for
k=1,2
, is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{A^{(k)}_{2,1}}\left({\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(t_{1},h_{1})}+{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(t_{2},h_{2})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=e^{-\lambda\pi\hat{h}^{2}_{k}},{}

where
\hat{h}_{k}
is determined in (
7.22
) as a function of
t_{1}-t_{2},h_{1},h_{2}
.

Using Lemma
7.21
, we can simplify (
7.44
) to get

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(k)}_{2,1}

\displaystyle=4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h_{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{t_{1}-t^{*}}e^{-\lambda\pi\hat{h}^{2}_{k}(t_{1}-t_{2},h_{1},h_{2})}\,{\rm d}t_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{1}\,{\rm d}t_{1}{}

(7.46)

\displaystyle\quad+4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{t_{1}}e^{-\lambda\pi\hat{h}^{2}_{k}(t_{1}-t_{2},h_{1},h_{2})}\,{\rm d}t_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{1}\,{\rm d}t_{1}.

Making the change of variable
t_{1}-t_{2}=t
in the expression of
\hat{h}_{k}(t_{1}-t_{2},h_{1},h_{2})
in (
7.46
) yields

\displaystyle\hskip-65.04256pt\Lambda^{(k)}_{2,1}

\displaystyle=4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h_{1}}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda\pi\hat{h}^{2}_{k}(t,h_{1},h_{2})}\,{\rm d}t\,{\rm d}h_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{1}{}

(7.47)

\displaystyle\qquad+4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda\pi\hat{h}^{2}_{k}(t,h_{1},h_{2})}\,{\rm d}t\,{\rm d}h_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{1}.

The total the handover frequency is

(7.48)

\lambda_{\mathcal{V}}=\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}\Lambda_{l}+2\sum_{l<r,k\in\{1,2\}}\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r}.

This completes the proof of Theorem
7.20
, provided Lemma
7.21
holds.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.21
.

For the two intersection points
(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{h}_{1}),(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
, given by (
7.12
) and (
7.22
), we have, for the event
A^{(k)}_{2,1}
with the new definition in (
7.45
),

(7.49)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{A^{(k)}_{2,1}}\left({\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(t_{1},h_{1})}+{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(t_{2},h_{2})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{(t_{1},h_{1}),(t_{2},h_{2})}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left((\hat{s}_{k},\hat{h}_{k})\text{ is a handover point}\right).

t
\bullet
(t_{2},h_{2})
\bullet
(t_{1},h_{1})
(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{h}_{1})
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}

1

The regions
E^{s_{1},v_{1}}_{\ell_{1}}
and
E^{s_{1},v_{2}}_{\ell_{1}}
must be empty of heads from
fast
and
slow
stations respectively, in case the mixed intersection
(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{h}_{1})
represents an handover.

t
\bullet
(t_{2},h_{2})
\bullet
(t_{1},h_{1})
(\hat{s}_{1},\ell_{1})
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}

2

The regions
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
and
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
must be empty of heads from
fast
and
slow
stations respectively, in case the mixed intersection
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
represents an handover.

Figure 7.9
.
Mixed handovers due to first and second intersection of birds of two types.

In the rest of the proof, we evaluate the probability in (
7.49
), for
k=1,2
. Let us now assume that
t_{1}>t_{2}
and
v_{1}>v_{2}
and consider that
(\hat{s}_{k},\hat{h}_{k})
is a handover point. The upper half ellipses corresponding to
(\hat{s}_{k},\hat{h}_{k})
are given by,

(7.50)

v_{1}^{2}(t-\hat{s}_{k})^{2}+h^{2}=\hat{h}_{k}^{2}\text{ and }v_{2}^{2}(t-\hat{s}_{k})^{2}+h^{2}=\hat{h}_{k}^{2}

and the corresponding regions are
E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}
and
E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}
, respectively. For a point
(\hat{s}_{k},\hat{h}_{k})
to be a handover point, we need that the regions
E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}
and
E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}
be empty of points from
{\mathcal{H}}^{1}
and
{\mathcal{H}}^{2}
, respectively (see Figure
7.9
). Since
{\mathcal{H}}^{1},{\mathcal{H}}^{2}
are Poisson point processes, the corresponding probability is

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{(t_{1},h_{1}),(t_{2},h_{2})}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left((\hat{s}_{k},\hat{h}_{k})\text{ is a handover point}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}}\left({\mathcal{H}}^{l}(E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{k}})=0\right){}

(7.51)

\displaystyle=\prod_{l=1,2}e^{-2\lambda_{l}v_{l}\frac{\pi\hat{h}_{k}^{2}}{2v_{l}}}=e^{-\pi\hat{h}_{k}^{2}(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})}=e^{-\pi\lambda\hat{h}_{k}^{2}},

since
\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}=\lambda
.
∎

Remark 7.22
.

We cannot directly recover the handover frequency for the single-speed case from the two-speed case. However we can use part of the above proof to recover the result. For this assume that,
v_{1}=v_{2}=v
,
\lambda_{1}=p\lambda,\lambda_{2}=(1-p)\lambda
, for some
p\in[0,1]
, then the sum of the first two term in (
7.47
) is

\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2}=\frac{4v\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}\left(p^{2}+(1-p)^{2}\right)
. The rest of the contribution concerns the mixed handover frequency as a sum
\sum_{l\neq r,k\in\{1,2\}}\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r}=\Lambda_{1,2}+\Lambda_{2,1}
, where
\Lambda^{(2)}_{1,2}=\Lambda_{1,2}
,
\Lambda^{(1)}_{2,1}=\Lambda_{2,1}
,
\Lambda^{(1)}_{1,2}=0
and
\Lambda^{(2)}_{2,1}=0
, since both birds are of the same type and hence intersect exactly once. Using the equality
\Lambda_{1,2}=\Lambda_{2,1}
, we have in total
\sum_{l\neq r,k\in\{1,2\}}\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r}=2\Lambda_{2,1}
, where

\displaystyle\Lambda_{2,1}

\displaystyle=4p(1-p)\lambda^{2}v^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda\pi\hat{h}^{2}}\,{\rm d}t\,{\rm d}h_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{1},{}

where,
\hat{h}^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\left[v^{2}t^{2}+2(h_{1}^{2}+h_{2}^{2})+\frac{(h_{2}^{2}-h_{1}^{2})^{2}}{v^{2}t^{2}}\right]
, given
t_{1}-t_{2}=t
and
h_{1},h_{2}
. We employ the usual analysis as earlier in (
7.34
) to get

\displaystyle\Lambda_{2,1}

\displaystyle=p(1-p)\times 4\lambda^{2}v^{2}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{3}}e^{-\lambda\pi\hat{h}^{2}}\,{\rm d}t\,{\rm d}h_{2}\,{\rm d}h_{1}=p(1-p)\frac{4v\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}.{}

In total we have

\lambda_{\mathcal{V}}=\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2}+2\Lambda_{2,1}=\frac{4v\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}\left(p^{2}+(1-p)^{2}\right)+2p(1-p)\frac{4v\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}=\frac{4v\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi},

which is indeed the handover frequency in the single-speed case.

Remark 7.23

(
\lambda_{1}=\lambda/2=\lambda_{2}
)
.

Consider the case of equal proportions of stations of different speeds, i.e.,
\lambda_{1}=\lambda/2=\lambda_{2}
. Then, from (
7.47
), we can simplify the term
\Lambda^{(k)}_{2,1}
for
k=1,2
as

(7.52)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\Lambda^{(k)}_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{2}v_{1}v_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\left[\int_{0}^{h_{1}}\!\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}e^{-\pi\lambda\hat{h}_{k}^{2}}{\rm d}t\,{\rm d}h_{2}+\int_{h_{1}}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\pi\lambda\hat{h}_{k}^{2}}{\rm d}t\,{\rm d}h_{2}\right]{\rm d}h_{1},

where
\hat{h}_{k}\equiv\hat{h}_{k}(t,h_{1},h_{2})
is derived in (
7.22
) for both
k=1,2
. The total handover frequency is

\lambda_{\mathcal{V}}=\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}(v_{1}+v_{2})+2\Lambda^{(1)}_{2,1}+2\Lambda^{(2)}_{2,1}.

7.2.
Multi-speed case

Our techniques naturally lift to the finite set of speeds case, except for a small adjustment required to quantify the effect of stations of all the different speeds on handovers of the same or mixed types. Suppose the set of speeds is
v_{[n]}:=\{v_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
, with the order
v_{1}>v_{2}>\cdots>v_{n}
and initial locations of stations are independent Poisson point processes
\tilde{\Phi}^{l}=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\delta_{(R^{(l)}_{i},\alpha^{(l)}_{i})}
, for
l\in[n]
.

7.2.1.
Head point process and radial bird particle process

For different speeds
v_{l}
with
l\in[n]
, consider
n
different head point processes for
l\in[n]
,

{\mathcal{H}}^{l}:=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\delta_{\left(T^{(l)}_{i},H^{(l)}_{i}\right)},\text{ where }\left(T^{(l)}_{i},H^{(l)}_{i}\right):=\left(-\frac{R^{(l)}_{i}}{v_{l}}\cos\alpha^{(l)}_{i},R^{(l)}_{i}|\sin\alpha^{(l)}_{i}|\right).

The following result is similar to Lemma
7.1
.

Lemma 7.24
.

For each
l\in[n]
,
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
is a Poisson point process on
\mathbb{H}^{+}
with intensity measure
\nu_{l}
where
d\nu_{l}:=v_{l}{\rm d}t\otimes 2\lambda_{l}{\rm d}h
. For
l\in[n]
, the point processes
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
, are independent.

We shall write
{\mathcal{H}}=\sum_{l\in[n]}{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
, the superposition of
n
independent Poisson point processes
\{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\}_{l\in[n]}
. We write the law of the head point process
{\mathcal{H}}=\sum_{l\in[n]}{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
, as a product law
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{H}}:=\bigotimes_{l\in[n]}\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}}
, since
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
’s are independent among each other. The radial bird particle process can be constructed as the sum
{\mathcal{P}}_{c}(v_{[n]}):=\sum_{l\in[n]}{\mathcal{P}}_{c}(v_{l})
, where,

{\mathcal{P}}_{c}(v_{l})=\sum_{i}\delta_{C_{i}^{l}},

where the support is the set of closed subsets of
\mathbb{H}^{+}
of the form (
7.3
). The Poisson structure and time-stationarity of
{\mathcal{P}}_{c}(v_{[n]})
is also preserved in this case, as seen in Lemma
7.2
for the two-speed case. In view of Remark
4.12
, we can equivalently consider
{\mathcal{P}}_{c}(v_{[n]})
as the marked head point point process
{\mathcal{H}}_{c}(v_{[n]})=\sum_{l\in[n]}{\mathcal{H}}_{c}(v_{l})
with closed set valued marks, where

{\mathcal{H}}_{c}(v_{l})=\sum_{i}\delta_{\left(T^{(l)}_{i},H^{(l)}_{i}\right),C_{i}^{l}}.

7.2.2.
Lower envelope process

The
lower envelope process
is a stochastic process on
\mathbb{H}^{+}
, is defined as
\{(t,L_{n}(t))\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
, where

L_{n}(t):=\min_{l\in[n]}\inf_{i\in\mathbb{N}}f_{v_{l}}\left(\left(R^{(l)}_{i},\alpha^{(l)}_{i}\right),t\right).

The lower envelope is also time-stationary in view of Lemma
7.3
for two-speed case. The
lower envelope
is defined as a random closed subset of
\mathbb{H}^{+}
as

{\mathcal{L}}_{e}(v_{[n]}):=\{(t,L_{n}(t)):t\in\mathbb{R}\}.

Let us define the open region above the lower envelope
{\mathcal{L}}_{e}(v_{[n]})
as

(7.53)

{\mathcal{L}}^{+}_{e}(v_{[n]}):=\{(t,h)\in\mathbb{H}^{+}\,\mbox{:}\,h>L_{n}(t)\}.

In the multi-speed case, we have a
half-ellipse condition
, analogous to Lemma
7.4
, the characterization for a point to be on the lower envelope
{\mathcal{L}}_{e}(v_{[n]})
. We shall state the result without proof.

Lemma 7.25

(Half-ellipse condition)
.

For any point
(s,u)\in\cup_{k\in[n]}\cup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}C^{l}_{i}
the following holds true:

(i).

(s,u)\in{\mathcal{L}}_{e}(v_{[n]})
if and only if
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}(E^{s,v_{l}}_{u})=0
for all
l\in[n]
;

(ii).

the probability of such an event is

(7.54)

\mathbb{P}\left((s,u)\in{\mathcal{L}}_{e}(v_{[n]})\right)=e^{-\lambda\pi u^{2}},

where
\lambda:=\sum_{l\in[n]}\lambda_{l}
.

7.2.3.
The handover point process and it’s intensity

In view of the construction of the handover point process in the two-speed case in Subsubsection
7.1.7
, we can also define the handover point process in the multi-speed case using the multi-speed radial bird particle process
{\mathcal{H}}_{c}(v_{[n]})
. By Lemma
7.25
, for the intersection point
(\hat{s},\hat{h})
of two radial birds with their heads at
(t,h),(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
give rise to a handover if and only if
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}(E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}})=0
, for all
l\in[n]
.

Formally, taking inspiration from the two-speed case in Subsubsection
7.1.7
, we can construct different handover point processes
{\mathcal{V}}_{l}
corresponding pure handovers of type
l
, for
l\in[n]
, as in (
7.25
), and
{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{l,r}
corresponding to mixed handovers of type
\binom{k}{l,r}
, for
l\neq r\in[n],k\in\{1,2\}
, as in (
7.26
), (
7.27
). Here, we recall that this type of mixed handover is due to the
k
-th intersection of the radial birds of types
l,r
such that
l\neq r
and the head point of the former is on the left of that of the latter.

Based on all possible types of pure and mixed intersections, while respecting the
intersection criterion
determined in Lemma
7.5
and Lemma
7.9
, the handover point process
{\mathcal{V}}
, similar to (
7.28
), is defined as

(7.55)

{\mathcal{V}}:=\sum_{l\in[n]}{\mathcal{V}}_{l}+\sum_{l\neq r\in[n],k=1,2}{\mathcal{V}}_{l,r}^{(k)}.

In the following, we just state the result about the time stationarity of the handover point process
{\mathcal{V}}
, which is inherited from the stationarity of the head point process
{\mathcal{H}}
.

Lemma 7.26

(Time-stationarity of
{\mathcal{V}}
)
.

In the multi-speed case, the handover point process
{\mathcal{V}}
is stationary with respect to time.

The construction of the point processes
{\mathcal{R}}_{l}
for
l\in[n]
and
{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{l,r}
for
l\neq r\in[n],k\in\{1,2\}
, corresponding to the right most head points is also the same and so is the existence of the bijections as in Lemma
7.18
.

Lemma 7.27
.

(i).

For all
l\in[n]
, there exists a bijection
\beta_{l}
between
{\mathcal{V}}_{l}
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{l}
.

(ii).

For all
l\neq r\in[n],k\in\{1,2\}
, there exists a bijection
\beta^{(k)}_{l,r}
between
{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{l,r}
and
{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{l,m}
.

We obtain the equality of intensities as a corollary:

Corollary 7.28
.

(i).

For
l\in[n]
, the intensity of the point processes
{\mathcal{V}}_{l}
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{l}
are equal,
\lambda_{{\mathcal{R}}_{l}}=\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l}}=\Lambda_{l}
.

(ii).

For all
l\neq r\in[n],k\in\{1,2\}
, the intensity of the point processes
{\mathcal{V}}_{l,r}^{(k)}
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{l,r}^{(k)}
are equal,
\lambda_{{\mathcal{R}}_{l,r}^{(k)}}=\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l,r}^{(k)}}=\Lambda_{l,r}^{(k)}
.

In the multi-speed scenario, the handover frequency can be written as a sum of pure and mixed handovers

\lambda_{\mathcal{V}}=\sum_{l\in[n]}\Lambda_{l}+\sum_{l\neq r\in[n],k\in\{1,2\}}\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r},

as described in the following result which generalizes Theorem
4.26
and Theorem
7.20
.

Theorem 7.29
.

The handover frequency in multi-speed case with set of speeds
v_{[n]}
is given by:

(1)

The pure handover frequencies are

(7.56)

\Lambda_{i}=\frac{4v_{i}\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}\left(\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda}\right)^{2},\text{ for }i\in[n].

(2)

For any pair
i\neq j\in[n]
, the mixed handover frequencies satisfies,
\Lambda^{(1)}_{i,j}=\Lambda^{(2)}_{j,i}
and
\Lambda^{(2)}_{i,j}=\Lambda^{(1)}_{i,j}
.

(3)

For any pair
j<i\in[n]
and
k\in\{1,2\}
,

(7.57)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(k)}_{i,j}

\displaystyle=4\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}v_{i}v_{j}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{j}}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda\pi\hat{h}^{2}_{k}(t,h_{j},h_{i})}\,{\rm d}t\,{\rm d}h_{i}+\int_{h_{j}}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda\pi\hat{h}^{2}_{k}(t,h_{j},h_{i})}\,{\rm d}t\,{\rm d}h_{i}\right]{\rm d}h_{j},

where

\hat{h}^{2}_{k}(t,h_{j},h_{i})=v_{j}^{2}\left(\frac{v_{i}^{2}t+(-1)^{k}\sqrt{\Delta_{i,j}}}{v_{j}^{2}-v_{i}^{2}}\right)^{2}+h_{j}^{2}\text{ and }\Delta_{i,j}=v_{j}^{2}v_{i}^{2}t^{2}-(h_{j}^{2}-h_{i}^{2})(v_{j}^{2}-v_{i}^{2}),

both of them as a function of
t,h_{j},h_{i}
. The quantity

t^{*}\equiv t^{*}(h_{j},h_{i}):=\frac{1}{v_{j}v_{i}}(h_{j}^{2}-h_{i}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{j}^{2}-v_{i}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}},

is the non-negative solution of
\Delta_{i,j}=0
.

Proof of Theorem
7.29
, part (
1
).

In the first part, the intensity of pure handovers is computed similar to (
7.34
) in the proof of two-speed case, as

(7.58)

\Lambda_{i}=\frac{4v_{i}\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}\left(\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda}\right)^{2},

for each
i\in[n]
, where
\lambda=\sum_{i\in[n]}\lambda_{i}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.29
, part (
2
).

Let
i>j\in[n]
and assume that
v_{j}>v_{i}
. We are interested in the handover between two stations with speed
v_{j},v_{i}
, which we call mixed handovers.
For such mixed handovers, by the symmetry argument as in the two-speed case in the proof of Part (
ii
) Theorem
7.20
, we have that
\Lambda^{(1)}_{i,j}=\Lambda^{(2)}_{j,i}
and
\Lambda^{(2)}_{i,j}=\Lambda^{(1)}_{j,i}
for all
j<i\in[n]
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.29
, part (
3
).

We just evaluate
\Lambda^{(k)}_{i,j}
for
j<i\in[n]
and
k=1,2
for handovers of type
\binom{k}{i,j}
. Let us straight away start from Equation (
7.42
) for the two-speed case in Theorem
7.20
and write the mixed handover frequency
\Lambda^{(k)}_{i,j}
as an integral by applying the Campbell-Mecke formula for the factorial moment measure of order 2 for the sum
{\mathcal{H}}:=\sum_{i\in[n]}{\mathcal{H}}^{i}
, as

(7.59)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(k)}_{i,j}\!

\displaystyle=\!4\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}v_{i}v_{j}\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\!\int_{D_{\ell}(t_{j},h_{j},h_{i})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathbb{E}^{(i,j)}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\!\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{A^{(k)}_{i,j}}\!\Big({\mathcal{H}}^{i}+\delta_{(t_{i},h_{i})}+{\mathcal{H}}^{j}+\delta_{(t_{j},h_{j})}+\!\!\sum_{m\neq i,j}{\mathcal{H}}^{m}\Big)\right]{\rm d}t_{i}{\rm d}h_{i}{\rm d}h_{j}{\rm d}t_{j}.

Here
A^{(k)}_{i,j}
is the event that there is a handover of type
\binom{k}{i,j}
at
\left(\hat{s}_{i,j}^{(k)},\hat{h}_{i,j}^{(k)}\right)
, for
k=1,2
, due to the intersection of the radial birds that have their heads at
(t_{j},h_{j})
and
(t_{i},h_{i})
, with
t_{i}<t_{j}
. Above, we took the expectation inside the integral under the two-point Palm probability measure

\mathbb{P}^{(i,j),(t_{i},h_{i}),(t_{j},h_{j})}_{{\mathcal{H}}}:=\mathbb{P}^{(t_{i},h_{i})}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{i}}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{(t_{j},h_{j})}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{j}}\otimes\bigotimes_{m\neq i,j}\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{m}}.

Let us evaluate the term
\Lambda^{(k)}_{i,j}
similarly to the two-speed case as

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(k)}_{i,j}

\displaystyle=4\lambda_{j}\lambda_{i}v_{j}v_{i}\int_{0}^{1}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\int_{0}^{h_{j}}\!\!\int_{-\infty}^{t_{r}-t^{*}}\!\!\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{A^{(k)}_{i,j}}\Big({\mathcal{H}}^{i}+\delta_{(t_{i},h_{i})}+{\mathcal{H}}^{j}+\delta_{(t_{j},h_{j})}+\!\!\sum_{m\neq i,j}{\mathcal{H}}^{m}\Big)\right]{\rm d}t_{i}\,{\rm d}h_{i}\,{\rm d}h_{j}\,{\rm d}t_{j}{}

(7.60)

\displaystyle\quad+4\lambda_{j}\lambda_{i}v_{j}v_{i}\int_{0}^{1}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\int_{h_{j}}^{\infty}\!\!\int_{-\infty}^{t_{j}}\!\!\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{A^{(k)}_{i,j}}\Big({\mathcal{H}}^{i}+\delta_{(t_{i},h_{i})}+{\mathcal{H}}^{j}+\delta_{(t_{j},h_{j})}+\!\!\sum_{m\neq i,j}{\mathcal{H}}^{m}\Big)\right]{\rm d}t_{i}\,{\rm d}h_{i}\,{\rm d}h_{j}\,{\rm d}t_{j},

where
t^{*}
is defined similarly to (
7.15
):

(7.61)

t^{*}\equiv t^{*}(h_{j},h_{i}):=\frac{1}{v_{j}v_{i}}(h_{j}^{2}-h_{i}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{j}^{2}-v_{i}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The quantity
t^{*}
is well defined, since
v_{j}>v_{i}
. In (
7.60
), we have used the fact that
t_{i}\leq t_{j}-t^{*}
if
h_{i}<h_{j}
and
t_{i}\leq t_{j}
, for
h_{i}\geq h_{j}
.

t
\bullet
(t_{i},h_{i})
\bullet
(t_{j},h_{j})
(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{h}_{1})
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{r}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}

Figure 7.10
.
We consider
(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{h}_{1})=(\hat{s}_{i,j}^{(1)},\hat{h}_{i,j}^{(1)})
,
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})=(\hat{s}_{i,j}^{(2)},\hat{h}_{i,j}^{(2)})
for simplicity. The mixed intersection
(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{h}_{1})
represents a handover if the regions
E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{i}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
are empty of heads from
{\mathcal{H}}^{i}
for all
i\in[n]
. The red and blue ellipses are for the speeds
v_{j}
and
v_{i}
, respectively and the green and black ones correspond to other speeds
v\neq v_{i},v_{j}
.

Under the two-point Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{(i,j),(t_{i},h_{i}),(t_{j},h_{j})}_{{\mathcal{H}}}
, we have

(7.62)

\displaystyle\!\!\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{A^{(k)}_{i,j}}\Big({\mathcal{H}}^{j}+\delta_{(t_{j},h_{j})}+{\mathcal{H}}^{i}+\delta_{(t_{i},h_{i})}+\!\!\sum_{m\neq i,j}{\mathcal{H}}^{m}\Big)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{(i,j),(t_{i},h_{i}),(t_{j},h_{j})}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\!\!\left(A^{(k)}_{i,j}\right).

Applying the Slivnyak-Mecke theorem and using Figure
7.10
, the term in (
7.62
) evaluates to

(7.63)

\displaystyle\prod_{m\in[n]}\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{m}}\left({\mathcal{H}}^{m}(E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{m}}_{\hat{h}_{k}})=0\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{m\in[n]}e^{-2\lambda_{m}v_{m}\frac{\pi\hat{h}_{k}^{2}}{2v_{m}}}=e^{-\pi\hat{h}_{k}^{2}\sum_{m\in[n]}\lambda_{m}}=e^{-\pi\lambda\hat{h}_{k}^{2}},

since
\sum_{m\in[n]}\lambda_{m}=\lambda
, where
(\hat{s}_{k},\hat{h}_{k})=(\hat{s}_{k}(t_{i},h_{i},t_{j},h_{j}),\hat{h}_{k}(t_{i},h_{i},t_{j},h_{j}))\equiv(\hat{s}_{k}(t_{j}-t_{i},h_{i},h_{j}),\hat{h}_{k}(t_{j}-t_{i},h_{i},h_{j}))
, the
k
-th intersection point of the radial birds at
(t_{i},h_{i}),(t_{j},h_{j})
. For any pair
i,j\in[n]
, with
j<i
and
k=1,2
, the mixed handover frequency is

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(k)}_{i,j}

\displaystyle=4\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}v_{i}v_{j}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h_{j}}\int_{-\infty}^{t_{j}-t^{*}}e^{-\pi\lambda\hat{h}_{k}^{2}(t_{j}-t_{i},h_{j},h_{i})}\;{\rm d}t_{i}\,{\rm d}h_{i}\,{\rm d}h_{j}\,{\rm d}t_{j}{}

\displaystyle\quad+4\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}v_{i}v_{j}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h_{j}}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{t_{i}}e^{-\pi\lambda\hat{h}_{k}^{2}(t_{j}-t_{i},h_{j},h_{i})}\;{\rm d}t_{i}\,{\rm d}h_{i}\,{\rm d}h_{j}\,{\rm d}t_{j}{}

(7.64)

\displaystyle=4\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}v_{i}v_{j}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{j}}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}e^{-\pi\lambda\hat{h}_{k}^{2}(t,h_{j},h_{i})}\;{\rm d}t\,{\rm d}h_{i}+\int_{h_{j}}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\pi\lambda\hat{h}_{k}^{2}(t,h_{j},h_{i})}\;{\rm d}t\,{\rm d}h_{i}\right]{\rm d}h_{j},

which is the required expression for
\Lambda^{(k)}_{i,j}
, where
t^{*}\equiv t^{*}(h_{j},h_{i}):=\frac{1}{v_{j}v_{i}}(h_{j}^{2}-h_{i}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{j}^{2}-v_{i}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
. From (
7.58
) and (
7.64
) and part (
2
), we get the value of total handover frequency

\lambda_{\mathcal{V}}=\sum_{i\in[n]}\Lambda_{i}+2\sum_{j<i\in[n],k=1,2}\Lambda^{(k)}_{i,j}.

This completes the proof of Theorem
7.29
.
∎

8.
Handover Palm distribution and applications: two-speed case

In this section, we focus again on the two-speed case. We first determine the Palm probability measure with respect to handovers with the goal to determine the Palm probability distribution of inter-handover times. An identical analysis allows one to come up with a result similar to the mass transport principle (Lemma
5.2
) for the two-speed case. In these initial steps, we derive the results in the two-speed case, as a first step towards the multi-speed case.

8.1.
Palm probability with respect to handovers

Without loss of generality, let us assume that
v_{1}>v_{2}
. Like in the single-speed case, we consider that the reference probability space is
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}},\mathbb{P})
,
with
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}})
the canonical space of head point process on
\mathbb{H}^{+}
. Each
\omega\in\Omega
is a realization of the point process

{\mathcal{H}}=\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
. We equip this measure space with the shift
\{\theta_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
, along the time axis, defined as

(8.1)

\theta_{t}({\mathcal{H}})=\theta_{t}({\mathcal{H}}^{1})+\theta_{t}({\mathcal{H}}^{2})=\sum_{i}\delta_{(T^{(1)}_{i}-t,H^{(1)}_{i})}+\sum_{i}\delta_{(T^{(2)}_{i}-t,H^{(2)}_{i})}.

The law
\mathbb{P}
of the head point process
{\mathcal{H}}
is left invariant by this shift and the latter is also ergodic for
\mathbb{P}
.
The handover point process
{\mathcal{V}}
is constructed as in Subsubsection
7.1.7
, more precisely as in (
7.28
) using the pure handover point processes
{\mathcal{V}}_{l}
, for
l=1,2
, and the mixed handover point processes
{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{l,m}
, for
l\neq m,k\in\{1,2\}
. The stationarity, or in other words
\theta_{t}
-compatibility of
{\mathcal{V}}
, is established in Lemma
7.17
, and
{\mathcal{V}}
has a positive and finite intensity from Theorem
7.20
. We denote the Palm probability w.r.t.
{\mathcal{V}}
on the probability space
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}})
, as
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}^{0}
. In what follows, we describe a variant of the mass transport principle, Lemma
5.2
, for the two-speed case.

Recall the point processes
{\mathcal{R}}_{l}
, for
l\in\{1,2\}
, and
{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{l,r}
, for
l\neq r,k\in\{1,2\}
, corresponding to the right-most head points for different types of handovers. The same mass transport principle, Lemma
5.2
, under the bijections defined in Lemma
7.18
applies to all the individual Palm probability measures, stated in the following.

Lemma 8.1
.

For all non-negative measurable functions
f
on
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}})
, we have:

(i).

For all
l\in\{1,2\}
,

(8.2)

\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{l}}^{0}\left[f(\theta_{\beta_{l}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right],

where
\beta_{l}
is the bijection between the point processes
{\mathcal{V}}_{l}
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{l}
, corresponding to handovers of pure type
l
.

(ii).

For all
l\neq r,k\in\{1,2\}
,

(8.3)

\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{l,r}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{l,r}}^{0}\left[f(\theta_{\beta^{(k)}_{l,r}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right],

where
\beta^{(k)}_{l,r}
is the bijection between the point processes
{\mathcal{V}}_{l,r}^{(k)}
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{l,r}^{(k)}
, corresponding to handovers of mixed type
\binom{k}{l,r}
.

Hence, we have the following decomposition of
\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]
, for any non-negative measurable function
f
on
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}})
, based on these different types of handovers using the Palm probability measure for the sum of multi-type independent point processes (cf.
[
4
, Subsection 1.6, p. 36]
),

Theorem 8.2
.

For all non-negative measurable functions
f
on
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}})
,

(8.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]=\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}\frac{\Lambda_{l}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]+\sum_{l\neq r,k\in\{1,2\}}\frac{\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r}}{\lambda_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l,r}^{(k)}}[f({\mathcal{H}})],

in terms of the pure and mixed Palm probability measures
\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l}}^{0}
, for
l\in\{1,2\}
, and
\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{l,r}}^{0}
, for
l\neq r,k\in\{1,2\}
.

Using the Poisson structure of
{\mathcal{H}}
, the pure handover Palm probability measures
\mathbb{P}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l}}
, for
l=1,2
, are expressed as follows, similarly to Theorem
5.7
:

Lemma 8.3
.

For
l=1,2
and for all non-negative measurable functions
f
on
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]

\displaystyle=\frac{4\lambda_{l}^{2}v_{l}^{2}}{\Lambda_{l}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{3}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[f\left(\theta_{\hat{s}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\left({\mathcal{H}}^{l}+\delta_{(0,h)}+\delta_{(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}+{\mathcal{H}}^{m}\right)\right)\prod_{i=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{i}\left(E^{\hat{s}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{i}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h,

where,
m\neq l
and
(\hat{s}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),\hat{h}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}))

are the coordinates of the intersection point of the two radial birds with some head points
(t,h)
and
(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
.

As a general principle, for any pair of head points
(t_{1},h_{1}),(t_{2},h_{2})\in\mathbb{H}^{+}
, the intersection point of the associated radial birds of the same type is denoted by
(\hat{s}(t_{1},h_{1},t_{2},h_{2}),\hat{h}(t_{1},h_{1},t_{2},h_{2}))
.
The intersection points are given by (
7.12
) and (
7.22
) if the corresponding birds are of different types and we denote them by
(\hat{s}_{k}(t_{1},h_{1},t_{2},h_{2}),\hat{h}_{k}(t_{1},h_{1},t_{2},h_{2}))
for
k=1,2
. The proof of Lemma
8.3
follows the same steps as those of Lemma
5.3
and of part (
ii
) of Lemma
8.1
, except for the extra condition on multiple half-ellipses being empty of head points corresponding to different speeds. Having said this, we skip the proof of the last result and move towards the more interesting case of mixed Palm probability measures
\mathbb{P}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l,r}^{(k)}}
, for
l,r,k\in\{1,2\}
with
l\neq r
, for which we provide more details in the following:

Lemma 8.4
.

For
l\neq r,k=1,2
and for all non-negative measurable functions
f
on
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}})
,

\displaystyle\frac{\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r}}{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{l,r}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\left(\theta_{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{r}+\delta_{(0,h)}+{\mathcal{H}}^{l}+\delta_{(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})})\right)\prod_{i=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{i}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{i}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

\displaystyle\,+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\left(\theta_{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{r}+\delta_{(0,h)}+{\mathcal{H}}^{l}+\delta_{(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})})\right)\prod_{i=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{i}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{i}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h,{}

where,
(\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),\hat{h}_{k}(0,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}))

are the coordinates of the
k
-th intersection of the two radial birds with heads
(0,h)
and
(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
of type
r
and
l
, respectively,
t^{*}=\frac{1}{v_{1}v_{2}}(h^{2}-h^{\prime 2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

Remark 8.5
.

Because of the integration range, the expression on the right integral does not include the region where there is no intersection of the radial birds with their heads at
(0,h)
and
(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
.

Proof of Lemma
8.4
,
l=2,r=1
and
k=1,2
.

Let us first derive the result for
l=2,r=1
and
k=1,2
, i.e., for
\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{2,1}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]
. The proof follows the same steps as those of the proof of Lemma
5.3
, under some adaptation. Suppose
(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i})
and
(T^{2}_{j},H^{2}_{j})
in the support of
{\mathcal{H}}^{1},{\mathcal{H}}^{2}
, respectively, such that
T_{j}^{2}\in D_{\ell}(T_{i}^{1},H_{i}^{1},H_{j}^{2})
. Let

(\hat{S}^{(k)}_{2,1},\hat{H}^{(k)}_{2,1})
, in short written as
(\hat{S}^{(k)},\hat{H}^{(k)})
, denote the coordinates of the
k
-th intersection of the two radial birds with these heads.
By (
8.3
) and the definition of
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{2,1}}^{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{2,1}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]=\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{2,1}}\left[f(\theta_{\beta^{(k)}_{2,1}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Lambda_{2,1}^{(k)}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{T^{1}_{i}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{2,1}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{1}_{i}\leq 1}f\left(\theta_{\beta^{(k)}_{2,1}(0)}\circ\theta_{T^{1}_{i}}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\right]{}

(8.5)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Lambda_{2,1}^{(k)}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{T^{1}_{i}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{2,1}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{1}_{i}\leq 1}f\left(\theta_{T^{1}_{i}+\beta^{(k)}_{2,1}(T^{1}_{i})}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\right],

using the fact that
\theta_{\beta^{(k)}_{2,1}(0)}\circ\theta_{T^{1}_{i}}=\theta_{T^{1}_{i}+\beta^{(k)}_{2,1}(T^{1}_{i})}
. The expectation in (
8.5
) can be evaluated as

(8.6)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{1}_{i}\leq 1}\!\!\!\!\!\mathbbmss{1}_{\exists(T^{2}_{j},H^{2}_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{2}_{j}\in D_{\ell}(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i},H^{2}_{j})}f\left(\theta_{T^{1}_{i}+\beta^{(k)}_{2,1}(T^{1}_{i})}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{S}^{(k)},v_{l}}_{\hat{H}^{(k)}}\right)=0}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{1}_{i}\leq 1}\,\sum_{(T^{2}_{j},H^{2}_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{2}_{j}\in D_{\ell}(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i},H^{2}_{j})}f\left(\theta_{T^{1}_{i}+\beta^{(k)}_{2,1}(T^{1}_{i})}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{S}^{(k)},v_{l}}_{\hat{H}^{(k)}}\right)=0}\right]{}

(8.7)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{1}_{i}\leq 1}\;\sum_{(T^{2}_{j},H^{2}_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{2}_{j}\in D_{\ell}(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i},H^{2}_{j})}f\left(\theta_{\hat{S}^{(k)}}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{S}^{(k)},v_{l}}_{\hat{H}^{(k)}}\right)=0}\right],

since
T^{1}_{i}+\beta^{(k)}_{2,1}(T^{1}_{i})=\hat{S}^{(k)}
. Applying the Campbell-Mecke formula for the factorial power of order 2 based on the two-point Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{(t,h),(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}_{{\mathcal{H}}}=\mathbb{P}^{(t,h)}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{1}}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}
, we can evaluate the expectation in (
8.7
) as

\displaystyle 4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{D_{\ell}(t,h,h^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}^{(t,h),(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\left(\theta_{\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}})\right)

(8.8)

\displaystyle\hskip 238.49121pt\times\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{D_{\ell}(t,h,h^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\left(\theta_{\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(t,h)}+{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})})\right){}

(8.9)

\displaystyle\hskip 238.49121pt\times\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{\rm d}t,

where the set
D_{\ell}(t,h,h^{\prime})
is defined as in (
7.8
). In case
h<h^{\prime}
, we have
D_{\ell}(t,h,h^{\prime})=(-\infty,t]
but in case
h\geq h^{\prime}
, we have
D_{\ell}(t,h,h^{\prime})=(-\infty,t-t^{*}]
, where
t^{*}
is as defined in (
7.15
). This simplifies the multiple integral in (
8.9
) to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{-\infty}^{t-t^{*}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\left(\theta_{\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(t,h)}+{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\left(\theta_{\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(t,h)}+{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{\rm d}t,{}

which can later be transformed to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\left(\theta_{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(0,h)}+{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h

(8.10)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\left(\theta_{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(0,h)}+{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h,

by making the change of variable
t-t^{\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
, where
t^{*}=\frac{1}{v_{1}v_{2}}(h^{2}-h^{\prime 2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
. We obtain the result for
l=2,r=1
and
k=1,2
, by combining the three equations (
8.7
), (
8.9
) and (
8.10
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.4
,
l=1,r=2
and
k=1,2
.

Here we derive the result, similarly to above for
l=2,r=1
and
k=1,2
, i.e., for
\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{1,2}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]
. Suppose
(T^{2}_{i},H^{2}_{i})
and
(T^{1}_{j},H^{1}_{j})
in the support of
{\mathcal{H}}^{2},{\mathcal{H}}^{1}
, respectively, such that
T_{j}^{1}\in D^{\prime}_{\ell}(T_{i}^{2},H_{i}^{2},H_{j}^{1})
. The
k
-th intersection point of the two radial birds with these heads is

(\hat{S}^{(k)}_{1,2},\hat{H}^{(k)}_{1,2})
, in short written as
(\hat{S}^{(k)},\hat{H}^{(k)})
.
By (
8.3
) and the definition of
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{1,2}}^{0}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{1,2}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]=\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{1,2}}\left[f(\theta_{\beta^{(k)}_{1,2}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Lambda_{1,2}^{(k)}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{T^{2}_{i}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{1,2}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{2}_{i}\leq 1}f\left(\theta_{\beta^{(k)}_{1,2}(0)}\circ\theta_{T^{2}_{i}}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\right]{}

(8.11)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Lambda_{1,2}^{(k)}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{T^{2}_{i}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{1,2}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{2}_{i}\leq 1}f\left(\theta_{T^{2}_{i}+\beta^{(k)}_{1,2}(T^{2}_{i})}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\right],

using the fact that
\theta_{\beta^{(k)}_{1,2}(0)}\circ\theta_{T^{2}_{i}}=\theta_{T^{2}_{i}+\beta^{(k)}_{1,2}(T^{2}_{i})}
. The last expectation in (
8.11
) is equal to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{T^{2}_{i}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{1,2}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{2}_{i}\leq 1}f\left(\theta_{T^{2}_{i}+\beta^{(k)}_{1,2}(T^{2}_{i})}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{(T^{2}_{i},H^{2}_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{2}_{i}\leq 1}\mathbbmss{1}_{\exists(T^{1}_{j},H^{1}_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{1}_{j}\in D^{\prime}_{\ell}(T^{2}_{i},H^{2}_{i},H^{1}_{j})}f\left(\theta_{T^{2}_{i}+\beta^{(k)}_{1,2}(T^{2}_{i})}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{S}^{(k)},v_{l}}_{\hat{H}^{(k)}}\right)=0}\right]{}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{(T^{2}_{i},H^{2}_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{2}_{i}\leq 1}\,\sum_{(T^{1}_{j},H^{1}_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{1}_{j}\in D^{\prime}_{\ell}(T^{2}_{i},H^{2}_{i},H^{1}_{j})}f\left(\theta_{T^{2}_{i}+\beta^{(k)}_{1,2}(T^{2}_{i})}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{S}^{(k)},v_{l}}_{\hat{H}^{(k)}}\right)=0}\right]{}

(8.12)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{(T^{2}_{i},H^{2}_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{2}_{i}\leq 1}\;\sum_{(T^{1}_{j},H^{1}_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{1}_{j}\in D^{\prime}_{\ell}(T^{2}_{i},H^{2}_{i},H^{1}_{j})}f\left(\theta_{\hat{S}^{(k)}}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{S}^{(k)},v_{l}}_{\hat{H}^{(k)}}\right)=0}\right],

where
T^{2}_{i}+\beta^{(k)}_{1,2}(T^{2}_{i})=\hat{S}^{(k)}
. Applying the Campbell-Mecke formula for the factorial power of order 2 based on the two-point Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{(t,h),(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}_{{\mathcal{H}}}=\mathbb{P}^{(t,h)}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{1}}
, the expectation in (
8.12
) evaluates to

\displaystyle 4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{D^{\prime}_{\ell}(t,h,h^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\left(\theta_{\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(t,h)}+{\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})})\right)

(8.13)

\displaystyle\hskip 238.49121pt\times\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{\rm d}t,

similarly to (
8.9
), where the set
D^{\prime}_{\ell}(t,h,h^{\prime})
is defined as in (
7.9
). The set
D^{\prime}_{\ell}(t,h,h^{\prime})=(-\infty,t]
when
h<h^{\prime}
but
D^{\prime}_{\ell}(t,h,h^{\prime})=(-\infty,t-t^{*}]
, when
h\geq h^{\prime}
, where
t^{*}
is as defined in (
7.15
). This leads to a simplification of the multiple integral in (
8.13
) to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{-\infty}^{t-t^{*}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\left(\theta_{\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(t,h)}+{\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\left(\theta_{\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(t,h)}+{\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{\rm d}t{}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\left(\theta_{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(0,h)}+{\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

(8.14)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\left(\theta_{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\delta_{(0,h)}+{\mathcal{H}}^{1}+\delta_{(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h,

by making the change of variable
t-t^{\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
, where
t^{*}=\frac{1}{v_{1}v_{2}}(h^{2}-h^{\prime 2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
. The result is obtained in this case by combining the three equations (
8.14
), (
8.13
) and (
8.12
).
∎

Remark 8.6
.

For the unit function, i.e.,
f({\mathcal{H}})=1
, we can check that
E_{{\mathcal{V}}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]=1
. Indeed, using Lemma
8.3
and part (
1
) of Theorem
7.29
we have

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]

\displaystyle=\frac{4\lambda_{l}^{2}v_{l}^{2}}{\Lambda_{l}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{3}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\prod_{i=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{i}\left(E^{\hat{s}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{i}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}t^{\prime}\,{\rm d}h^{\prime}\,{\rm d}h{}

(8.15)

\displaystyle=\frac{4\lambda_{l}^{2}v_{l}^{2}}{\Lambda_{l}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{3}}e^{-\lambda\pi\hat{h}^{2}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}{\rm d}t^{\prime}\,{\rm d}h^{\prime}\,{\rm d}h=\frac{4v_{l}\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi\Lambda_{l}}\left(\frac{\lambda_{l}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}=1.

Using Lemma
8.4
, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{l,r}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]

\displaystyle=\frac{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}{\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{0}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[\prod_{i=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{i}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{i}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}{\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[\prod_{i=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{i}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{i}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}{\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda\pi\hat{h}^{2}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}+\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda\pi\hat{h}^{2}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}\right]{\rm d}h{}

(8.16)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r}}\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r}=1,

from the integral in (
7.47
). Thus using Theorem
8.2
, Lemma
8.1
and (
7.48
), we have

\displaystyle E_{{\mathcal{V}}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}\Lambda_{l}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\sum_{l,r,k\in\{1,2\}:l\neq r}\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r}=1.{}

8.1.1.
Decomposition of
\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]
for symmetric functions
f

The time reversal argument also provides symmetric relations among the Palm probability measures
\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{l,r}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]
, for pairs of values of
l\neq r,k\in\{1,2\}
. It is described in the forth-coming result, under a necessary condition that the function
f
is
symmetric
, which we define as follows. Define
-{\mathcal{H}}:=\sum_{i}\delta_{(-T_{i},H_{i})}
. We say that a function
f:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
is said to be
symmetric
, if
f(-{\mathcal{H}})=f({\mathcal{H}})
.

Lemma 8.7
.

For all non-negative measurable and symmetric functions
f

(8.17)

\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(1)}_{1,2}}\left[f({\mathcal{H}})\right]=\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(2)}_{2,1}}\left[f({\mathcal{H}})\right]\text{ and }\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(2)}_{1,2}}\left[f({\mathcal{H}})\right]=\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(1)}_{2,1}}\left[f({\mathcal{H}})\right].

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{L}}_{1,2}^{(k)}
be the point process made of the abscissas of the left most head point corresponding to handovers of type
\binom{k}{1,2}
. Then, there exist bijections
\tilde{\beta}^{(k)}_{1,2}
between
{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{1,2}
and
{\mathcal{L}}^{(k)}_{1,2}
, defined similarly to
\beta^{(k)}_{1,2}
, for
k=1,2
, as in Part (
ii
) of Lemma
7.18
. Under the symmetry assumption on the measurable function
f
,
we have the following relations:

(8.18)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(1)}_{1,2}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}_{1,2}}\left[f(\theta_{\tilde{\beta}^{(1)}_{1,2}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right]=\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{R}}^{(2)}_{2,1}}\left[f(\theta_{\beta^{(2)}_{2,1}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right]=\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(2)}_{2,1}}[f({\mathcal{H}})],

(8.19)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(2)}_{1,2}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{(2)}_{1,2}}\left[f(\theta_{\tilde{\beta}^{(2)}_{1,2}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right]=\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{R}}^{(1)}_{2,1}}\left[f(\theta_{\beta^{(1)}_{2,1}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right]=\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(1)}_{2,1}}[f({\mathcal{H}})].

In both relations, the third equality can be justified through a time-reversal argument, similarly to (
7.41
, proved in the following lemma. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.8
.

For all non-negative measurable and symmetric functions
f
, we have

(8.20)

\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}_{1,2}}\left[f(\theta_{\tilde{\beta}^{(1)}_{1,2}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right]\!=\!\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{R}}^{(2)}_{2,1}}\left[f(\theta_{\beta^{(2)}_{2,1}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right],

(8.21)

\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{(2)}_{1,2}}\left[f(\theta_{\tilde{\beta}^{(2)}_{1,2}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right]\!=\!\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{R}}^{(1)}_{2,1}}\left[f(\theta_{\beta^{(1)}_{2,1}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right].

Proof.

For the equality in (
8.20
), we can write similarly to (
8.5
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}_{1,2}}\left[f(\theta_{\tilde{\beta}^{(1)}_{1,2}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Lambda_{1,2}^{(1)}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{T^{1}_{i}\in{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}_{1,2}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{1}_{i}\leq 1}f\left(\theta_{\tilde{\beta}^{(1)}_{1,2}(0)}\circ\theta_{T^{1}_{i}}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\right]{}

(8.22)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Lambda_{1,2}^{(1)}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{T^{1}_{i}\in{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}_{1,2}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{1}_{i}\leq 1}f\left(\theta_{T^{1}_{i}+\tilde{\beta}^{(1)}_{1,2}(T^{1}_{i})}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\right],

using the fact that
\theta_{\tilde{\beta}^{(1)}_{1,2}(0)}\circ\theta_{T^{1}_{i}}=\theta_{T^{1}_{i}+\tilde{\beta}^{(1)}_{1,2}(T^{1}_{i})}
. Thus from (
8.22
) we have

\displaystyle\!\!\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{T^{1}_{i}\in{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}_{1,2}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{1}_{i}\leq 1}f\left(\theta_{T^{1}_{i}+\tilde{\beta}^{(1)}_{1,2}(T^{1}_{i})}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{1}_{i}\leq 1}\!\!\!\mathbbmss{1}_{\exists(T^{2}_{j},H^{2}_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{2}_{j}\in D_{r}(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i},H^{2}_{j})}\;f\left(\theta_{T^{1}_{i}+\tilde{\beta}^{(1)}_{1,2}(T^{1}_{i})}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{S}^{(1)},v_{l}}_{\hat{H}^{(1)}}\right)=0}\right]{}

(8.23)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{1}_{i}\leq 1}\,\sum_{(T^{2}_{j},H^{2}_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{2}_{j}\in D_{r}(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i},H^{2}_{j})}\!\!f\left(\theta_{T^{1}_{i}+\tilde{\beta}^{(1)}_{1,2}(T^{1}_{i})}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{S}^{(1)},v_{l}}_{\hat{H}^{(1)}}\right)=0}\right].

Using the fact that
T^{1}_{i}+\tilde{\beta}^{(1)}_{1,2}(T^{1}_{i})=\hat{S}^{(1)}
we have (
8.23
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{T^{1}_{i}\in{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}_{1,2}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{1}_{i}\leq 1}f\left(\theta_{T^{1}_{i}+\tilde{\beta}^{(1)}_{1,2}(T^{1}_{i})}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{1}_{i}\leq 1}\;\sum_{(T^{2}_{j},H^{2}_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{2}_{j}\in D_{r}(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i},H^{2}_{j})}f\left(\theta_{\hat{S}^{(1)}}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{S}^{(1)},v_{l}}_{\hat{H}^{(1)}}\right)=0}\right]{}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,0\leq T^{1}_{i}\leq 1}\;\sum_{(T^{2}_{j},H^{2}_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T^{2}_{j}\in D_{\ell}(T^{1}_{i},H^{1}_{i},H^{2}_{j})}f\left(\theta_{\tilde{S}^{(2)}}({\mathcal{H}})\right)\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\tilde{S}^{(2)},v_{l}}_{\tilde{H}^{(2)}}\right)=0}\right]{}

(8.24)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq:Last-Hijj}),(\ref{eq:Last-Hijjj})}}{{=}}\Lambda_{2,1}^{(2)}\,\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{R}}^{(2)}_{2,1}}\left[f(\theta_{\beta^{(2)}_{2,1}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right],

where
(\tilde{S}^{(2)},\tilde{H}^{(2)})
is the intersection of type
\binom{2}{2,1}
. In the second step in (
8.24
), after counting backwards in time, we have used the symmetry of the function
f
, i.e.,
f({\mathcal{H}})=f(-{\mathcal{H}})
. As seen before in Part (
ii
) of Theorem
7.20
, we have
\Lambda_{1,2}^{(1)}=\Lambda_{2,1}^{(2)}
. We obtain the result using the last fact and combining (
8.22
) and (
8.24
). The equality in (
8.21
) can be proved similarly.
∎

Under this symmetry we can re-write the decomposition by folding the symmetric terms for mixed handovers in (
8.4
), as

(8.25)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]=\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}\frac{\Lambda_{l}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]+2\sum_{r<l,k\in\{1,2\}}\frac{\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r}}{\lambda_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l,m}^{(k)}}[f({\mathcal{H}})].

8.2.
Typical handover distance in the two-speed case

Let
\hat{H}
be the random variable for the distance of the typical handover. Based on different type of typical handovers, we have following result about the decomposition of the Laplace transform of
\hat{H}^{2}
under the Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{0}_{\mathcal{V}}
.

Lemma 8.9
.

under the Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{V}}^{0}
of handovers, for any parameter
\gamma\geq 0
, the Laplace transform of
\hat{H}^{2}
is

(8.26)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}^{0}_{\hat{H}^{2}}(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\frac{4\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{\lambda\pi}\right)^{-3/2}\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}v_{l}\left(\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}+\frac{8\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\sum_{k\in\{1,2\}}\eta^{(k)}_{2,1}(\gamma),

where, for
k=1,2
,
\eta^{(k)}_{2,1}(\gamma)
is determined in (
8.30
) and (
8.31
), respectively.

Proof of Lemma
8.9
.

We apply the decomposition of the Palm probability measure with respect to handovers as in Theorem
8.2
, with the help of Lemma
8.3
, Lemma
8.4
, for the symmetric function
f({\mathcal{H}})=e^{-\gamma\hat{H}^{2}}
. The decomposition is according to the different types of handovers. Let
\hat{H}_{l}
is the typical handover distance corresponding to the handover of pure type
l
, for
l\in\{1,2\}
. For
l\neq r,k\in\{1,2\}
,
\hat{H}_{l,r}^{(k)}
denotes the typical handover distance due to the typical handover of type
\binom{k}{l,r}
. Observation
7.14
supports the fact that

(8.27)

\hat{H}_{2,1}^{(1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\hat{H}_{1,2}^{(2)}\text{ and }\hat{H}_{2,1}^{(2)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\hat{H}_{1,2}^{(1)}.

Then using the symmetry in (
8.27
) and the formula (
8.25
), the Laplace transform of
\hat{H}^{2}
under the Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{0}_{\mathcal{V}}
is

(8.28)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{\mathcal{V}}\left[e^{-\gamma\hat{H}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}\frac{\Lambda_{l}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l}}\left[e^{-\gamma\hat{H}^{2}_{l}}\right]+2\sum_{k\in\{1,2\}}\frac{\Lambda^{(k)}_{2,1}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{2,1}}\left[e^{-\gamma(\hat{H}^{(k)}_{2,1})^{2}}\right].

for any parameter
\gamma\geq 0
. Using calculations similar to those in Equation (
7.34
) and Remark
5.7
, we can obtain that

(8.29)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l}}\left[e^{-\gamma\hat{H}^{2}_{l}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Lambda_{l}}\left(\frac{\lambda_{l}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}\frac{4v_{l}\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{\lambda\pi}\right)^{-3/2}=\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{\lambda\pi}\right)^{-3/2},

since
\Lambda_{l}=\left(\frac{\lambda_{l}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}\frac{4v_{l}\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}
. Thus we can say that, for the pure handover of type
l
, the handover distance
\hat{H}_{l}
follows a Nakagami distribution with parameters
\left(\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2\lambda\pi}\right)
, for each
l=1,2
, see Lemma
5.6
. In what follows, we evaluate the individual Laplace transforms
\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(1)}_{2,1}}\left[e^{-\gamma(\hat{H}^{(1)}_{2,1})^{2}}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(2)}_{2,1}}\left[e^{-\gamma(\hat{H}^{(2)}_{2,1})^{2}}\right]
, which is enough due to the symmetry in (
8.27
). Using the formula in Lemma
8.4
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{\Lambda_{2,1}^{(1)}}{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(1)}_{2,1}}\left[e^{-\gamma(\hat{H}^{(1)}_{2,1})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[e^{-\gamma\hat{h}^{2}_{1}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[e^{-\gamma\hat{h}^{2}_{1}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}e^{-(\gamma+\lambda\pi)\hat{h}^{2}_{1}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}\,{\rm d}h^{\prime}\,{\rm d}h+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-(\gamma+\lambda\pi)\hat{h}^{2}_{1}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}\,{\rm d}h^{\prime}\,{\rm d}h{}

(8.30)

\displaystyle:=\eta^{(1)}_{2,1}(\gamma),

where
t^{*}=\frac{1}{v_{1}v_{2}}(h^{2}-h^{\prime 2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
, given
h,h^{\prime}
. Similarly, we can rewrite the other term as

\displaystyle\frac{\Lambda_{2,1}^{(2)}}{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(2)}_{2,1}}\left[e^{-\gamma(\hat{H}^{(2)}_{2,1})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}e^{-(\gamma+\lambda\pi)\hat{h}^{2}_{2}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}\,{\rm d}h^{\prime}\,{\rm d}h+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-(\gamma+\lambda\pi)\hat{h}^{2}_{2}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}\,{\rm d}h^{\prime}\,{\rm d}h{}

(8.31)

\displaystyle:=\eta^{(2)}_{2,1}(\gamma),

where
t^{*}=\frac{1}{v_{1}v_{2}}(h^{2}-h^{\prime 2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
, given
h,h^{\prime}
. Observe that, taking
\gamma=0
in (
8.30
) and (
8.31
), and later using (
7.64
) we have

(8.32)

4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}\,\eta^{(k)}_{2,1}(0)=\Lambda_{2,1}^{(k)},

for
k=1,2
. The final expression for
{\mathcal{L}}^{0}_{\hat{H}^{2}}(\gamma)
can be obtained by substituting the resultant from (
8.29
), (
8.30
) and (
8.31
) to (
8.28
) as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}^{0}_{\hat{H}^{2}}(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}\frac{\Lambda_{l}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{\lambda\pi}\right)^{-3/2}+2\sum_{k\in\{1,2\}}\frac{\Lambda^{(k)}_{2,1}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\frac{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}{\Lambda_{2,1}^{(k)}}\eta^{(k)}_{2,1}(\gamma){}

(8.33)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\frac{4\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{\lambda\pi}\right)^{-3/2}\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}v_{l}\left(\frac{\lambda_{l}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}+\frac{8\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\sum_{k\in\{1,2\}}\eta^{(k)}_{2,1}(\gamma),

where
\Lambda_{l}=\left(\frac{\lambda_{l}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}\frac{4v_{l}\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}
, for
l=1,2
and
\eta^{(k)}_{2,1}(\gamma)
determined in (
8.30
) and(
8.31
), for
k=1,2
.
∎

Remark 8.10

(
{\mathcal{L}}^{0}_{\hat{H}^{2}}(0)=1
)
.

We can perform a sanity check that
{\mathcal{L}}^{0}_{\hat{H}^{2}}(0)=1
. Indeed, by taking
\gamma=0
in (
8.33
), we have,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}^{0}_{\hat{H}^{2}}(0)=\frac{1}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\frac{4\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}v_{l}\left(\frac{\lambda_{l}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}+\frac{8\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\sum_{k\in\{1,2\}}\eta^{(k)}_{2,1}(0)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}\Lambda_{l}+\frac{2}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\sum_{k\in\{1,2\}}\Lambda_{2,1}^{(k)}=1,{}

where we have used the fact that
4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}\,\eta^{(k)}_{2,1}(0)=\Lambda_{2,1}^{(k)}
from (
8.32
), for
k=1,2
,
\Lambda_{l}=\frac{4\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}v_{l}\left(\frac{\lambda_{l}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}
, for
l=1,2
and the total handover frequency derived in (
7.48
).

Remark 8.11

(Single-speed case)
.

Using the last result, we can recover the Laplace transform of
\hat{H}^{2}
under the Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{0}_{\mathcal{V}}
in the single-speed case, which is
{\mathcal{L}}^{0}_{\hat{H}^{2}}(\gamma)=\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{\lambda\pi}\right)^{-3/2}
, for any speed
v
, as shown in Lemma
5.6
. Take
v_{1}=v_{2}=v
and
\lambda_{1}=p\lambda
and
\lambda_{2}=(1-p)\lambda
with
p\in[0,1]
. In (
8.26
), there is contribution from just one of the pair of terms
(\eta^{(1)}_{2,1}(\gamma),\Lambda^{(1)}_{2,1})
and
(\eta^{(2)}_{2,1}(\gamma),\Lambda^{(2)}_{2,1})
, corresponding to the mixed handovers, since there is just one intersection between any two radial birds, almost surely. Let us denote the surviving term as
(\eta_{2,1}(\gamma),\Lambda_{2,1})
, where
\eta_{2,1}(\gamma)
can be determined from one of (
8.30
) and (
8.31
). Then from (
8.26
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}^{0}_{\hat{H}^{2}}(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\frac{4\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{\lambda\pi}\right)^{-3/2}v(p^{2}+(1-p)^{2})+\frac{8\lambda^{2}v^{2}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}p(1-p)\eta_{2,1}(\gamma){}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\frac{4\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{\lambda\pi}\right)^{-3/2}v(p^{2}+(1-p)^{2})+\frac{2}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}p(1-p)\frac{4v\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{\lambda\pi}\right)^{-3/2}{}

(8.34)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\frac{4v\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{\lambda\pi}\right)^{-3/2}(p^{2}+(1-p)^{2}+2p(1-p))=\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{\lambda\pi}\right)^{-3/2},

since
\lambda_{\mathcal{V}}=\frac{4v\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}
, in the single-speed case and
4\lambda^{2}v^{2}\eta_{2,1}(\gamma)=\frac{4v\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{\lambda\pi}\right)^{-3/2}
, as in (
8.29
), using the fact that
t^{*}=0
in one of (
8.30
) or (
8.31
).

8.3.
Distribution of inter-handover time: two-speed case

The entire subsection is devoted to our final result about the Palm probability distribution of the
inter-handover time

T
. It is based on an extension of the ideas in the proof of Theorem
5.17
, with the required adaptation due to the existence of different types of handovers at a typical time.
Under the Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}}
of handovers, the
inter-handover time
is the duration between the handover at time
0
and the next handover. We denote the inter-handover time by
T
. Before moving to the Palm probability distribution of the inter-handover time, we refer the reader to Subsubsection
7.1.4
and Subsubsection
7.1.5
for the geometric interpretation of the intersection criteria (Subsubsection
7.1.3
) and their applications. This enables us to properly manage the rise in the complexity resulting from the introduction of more than one speed.

8.3.1.
Inter-handover time

Suppose for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
T_{1}(s)
denotes the first time a handover happens after time
s
. To formally define the inter-handover time, suppose there is a handover at time
0
and at distance
\hat{h}_{0}
. For ease of usage, we introduce an alternative notation for handover types. We denote the head points of the radial birds corresponding to the station that takes over the service and leaves the service, by
(t_{n},h_{n})
and
(t_{p},h_{p})
, respectively. Here the subscript
n
stands for
next
and
p
stands for
previous
. We use the notation
{\tau}_{n}=type(n)
, for the type of the next head point, and
{\tau}_{p}=type(p)
, for the type of the previous head point. Let us define

(8.35)

q:=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }t_{n}\geq t_{p},\\
2&\text{ if }t_{n}<t_{p}.\end{cases}

Then we define

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n}\end{array}\bigg]

to be a class or type of a handover where the next serving station is of type
{\tau}_{n}
, the previous one is of type
{\tau}_{n}
and
q=1
or
2
, depending on whether
t_{n}\geq t_{p}
or
t_{n}<t_{p}
, respectively. ​​​ The correspondence between the notation used so far and the new one is given below. Consider the pure handovers of type
l
as
\binom{1}{l,l}
, for
l=1,2
. Then the bijection between the two notations is given in Table
8.1
.

.

and

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n}\end{array}\bigg]

. Here HoPP stands for handover point process.

Suppose the typical handover is created by the intersection of the radial birds
C^{{\tau}_{n}}_{(t_{n},h_{n})}
and
C^{{\tau}_{p}}_{(t_{p},h_{p})}
, for some head points
(t_{n},h_{n})
and
(t_{p},h_{p})
of type
{\tau}_{n}
and
{\tau}_{p}
, respectively, for some
{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{p}\in\{1,2\}
. Note that
{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{p}
can be equal. Beware that, unlike in Subsubsection
7.1.4
, we do not fix here the type of the head points
(t_{n},h_{n})
and
(t_{p},h_{p})
. The handover at time
0
is pure and of type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n}\end{array}\bigg]

, if
{\tau}_{p}={\tau}_{n}
. If
{\tau}_{p}\neq{\tau}_{n}
, these two birds can lead to a handover of any of the types
\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n}\end{array}\bigg]$},\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n}\end{array}\bigg]$}
, for
{\tau}_{p}\neq{\tau}_{n}\in\{1,2\}
. More precisely, for
{\tau}_{p}\neq{\tau}_{n}
with
{\tau}_{n}=1
or
2
, if
t_{n}<t_{p}
, then the handover is mixed and can be of type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
2,1\end{array}\bigg]

or

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
1,2\end{array}\bigg]

, and if
t_{n}\geq t_{p}
, then it can be of type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
1,2\end{array}\bigg]

or

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
2,1\end{array}\bigg]

.

From now onward, we only use the new notational system of

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{p}\end{array}\bigg]

for pure and mixed handovers. We write
v_{{\tau}_{n}}
and
v_{{\tau}_{p}}
in short as
v_{n}
and
v_{p}
, respectively and write
C^{n}_{(t_{n},h_{n})}
and
C^{p}_{(t_{p},h_{p})}
in short for
C^{{\tau}_{n}}_{(t_{n},h_{n})}
and
C^{{\tau}_{p}}_{(t_{p},h_{p})}
, respectively. Consider the set
\{\hat{h}_{n}(t)\}_{t\geq 0}
, where
\hat{h}_{n}(t)(\equiv\hat{h}_{{\tau}_{n}}(t)):=\left(v_{n}^{2}(t-t_{n})^{2}+h_{n}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
, denotes the height of the radial bird
C^{n}_{(t_{n},h_{n})}
at time
t
. Essentially, the curve
\hat{h}_{n}(\cdot)
, as a function of time, represents the curve of the radial bird
C^{{\tau}_{n}}_{(t_{n},h_{n})}
, which takes over. Observe that
\hat{h}_{n}(0)=(v_{n}^{2}t_{n}^{2}+h_{n}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}:=\hat{h}_{0}
.

For
m\in\{1,2\}
such that
m\neq{\tau}_{n}
, let
\left\{E^{t,v_{n}}_{\hat{h}_{n}(t)}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
and
\left\{E^{t,v_{m}}_{\hat{h}_{n}(t)}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
, be the two collections of open half-ellipses of heights
\{\hat{h}_{n}(t)\}_{t\geq 0}
. For
t\geq 0
, define the extra regions

(8.36)

S^{n}_{t}:={\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t^{\prime}\in[0,t]}E^{t^{\prime},v_{n}}_{\hat{h}_{n}(t^{\prime})}\setminus E^{0,v_{n}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}\text{ and }S^{m}_{t}:={\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t^{\prime}\in[0,t]}E^{t^{\prime},v_{m}}_{\hat{h}_{n}(t^{\prime})}\setminus E^{0,v_{m}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}.

Due to their definition as unions of sets, we have:

Lemma 8.12
.

For
m\neq{\tau}_{n}
, the collections of open sets
\left\{S^{n}_{t}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
and
\left\{S^{m}_{t}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
are both non-decreasing in
t
.

Definition 8.13

(
Inter-handover time)
.

Let
m\in\{1,2\}
be such that
m\neq{\tau}_{n}
. Then, the time of the next handover is defined as

(8.37)

\displaystyle\!T

\displaystyle:=\inf\Big\{s>0:\forall t\in(0,s],{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(S^{n}_{t})=0,{\mathcal{H}}^{m}(S^{m}_{t})=0\text{ and }{\mathcal{H}}^{n}\left(\overline{S^{n}_{s}}\right)=2\text{ or }{\mathcal{H}}^{m}\left(\overline{S^{m}_{s}}\right)=1\Big\},

which coincides with
T_{1}(0)
, the time point of the first handover happens after time
0
.

Here is the rationale for the definition of the inter-handover time
T=T_{1}(0)
, essentially under the Palm distribution. As a consequence of the increasing property of the collections
\left\{S^{n}_{t}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
and
\left\{S^{m}_{t}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
, presented in Lemma
8.12
, we can conclude that
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}\left(S^{n}_{T_{1}(0)}\right)=0
implies
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}\left(S^{n}_{t}\right)=0
, and
{\mathcal{H}}^{m}\left(S^{m}_{T_{1}(0)}\right)=0
implies
{\mathcal{H}}^{m}\left(S^{m}_{t}\right)=0
, for all
t\in[0,T_{1}(0)]
. On the other hand, by the definition of
T_{1}(0)
in (
8.37
), the condition that
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}\left(\overline{S^{n}_{T_{1}(0)}}\right)=2
or
{\mathcal{H}}^{m}\left(\overline{S^{m}_{T_{1}(0)}}\right)=1
essentially implies that there exists a head point
(t_{u},h_{u})
, where
u
stands for
upcoming
, of type
{\tau}_{u}={\tau}_{n}
or
m
such that
C^{n}_{(t_{n},h_{n})}
and
C^{u}_{(t_{u},h_{u})}
intersect at
\left(T_{1}(0),\hat{h}_{T_{1}(0)}\right)
,
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}\left(S^{n}_{T_{1}(0)}\right)=0
and
{\mathcal{H}}^{m}\left(S^{m}_{T_{1}(0)}\right)=0
, so that a handover happens at
T_{1}(0)
, and in addition to that, it is the first handover after
0
. Also, at
t=T_{1}(0)
, either
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}\left(\overline{S^{n}_{T_{1}(0)}}\right)=2
or
{\mathcal{H}}^{m}\left(\overline{S^{m}_{T_{1}(0)}}\right)=1
, depending on whether
(t_{u},h_{u})
is of type
{\tau}_{u}={\tau}_{n}
or type
{\tau}_{u}=m
.

The point
(t_{u},h_{u})
can again be the previously considered head point
(t_{p},h_{p})
in certain situations, where
{\tau}_{n}\neq{\tau}_{p}
, in which case we have
{\tau}_{u}={\tau}_{p}=m
and the condition
{\mathcal{H}}^{m}\left(\overline{S^{m}_{T_{1}(0)}}\right)=1
, in the definition of
T_{1}(0)
, is anyway met. This situation appears when the typical handover is given by the first intersection of two radial birds of different types and the next handover is given by the second intersection of the same pair. We refer the reader to Figure
8.8
and Figure
8.11
in the later part of this article, for better understanding of this scenario. Thus we also have the following corollary of Lemma
8.12
, similar to Corollary
5.16
, for the two-speed case:

Corollary 8.14
.

For the time
s
meeting the stopping condition in the definition of
T_{1}(0)
in (
8.37
), there exists an a.s. unique point
(t_{u},h_{u})\in\mathbb{H}^{+}
, such that the radial bird with a head at this point, intersects the radial bird
C^{n}_{(t_{n},h_{n})}
and produces the handover at time
T_{1}(0)
.

Remark 8.15
.

The specific sub-domain of
\mathbb{H}^{+}
where to encounter the head point in question above, will be determined in the individual cases of the Palm distribution, depending on the type of the handover. We call these sub-domains as
unexplored regions
.

8.3.2.
Monotonicity properties of the collection
\left\{E^{t,v_{n}}_{\hat{h}_{n}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{n}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq 0}

Recall the setting of Subsubsection
8.3.1
, where there exists a handover at time
0
such that a station corresponding to type
{\tau}_{n}
takes over as a serving station after time
0
.
In the following, we discuss the monotonicity properties of the collections
\left\{E^{t,v_{n}}_{\hat{h}_{n}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{n}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
and
\left\{E^{t,v_{m}}_{\hat{h}_{n}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{m}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
for
m\neq{\tau}_{n}
, under all circumstances.

Proposition 8.16
.

Under the foregoing assumption, we have the following monotonicity properties:

(i).

If
{\tau}_{n}=1
, both collections of sets
\left\{E^{t,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
and
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
, where
\hat{h}_{1}(t):=\left(v_{1}^{2}(t-t_{n})^{2}+h_{n}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
, are non-decreasing in
t
.

(ii).

If
{\tau}_{n}=2
, the collection of sets
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
, where
\hat{h}_{2}(t):=\left(v_{2}^{2}(t-t_{n})^{2}+h_{n}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
, is non-decreasing, but the collection of sets
\left\{E^{t,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
is not always monotonic in
t
.

We provide a proof of this monotonicity in Appendix
A.3
, with the help of the

increasing wing property
discussed below for the two-speed case, similar to Remark
5.14
for the single speed case.

Remark 8.17

(Increasing wing property)
.

Under the Palm distribution of handovers, let
(0,\hat{h}_{0})\in C^{n}_{(t_{n},h_{n})}
. Define
\hat{h}_{n}(t):=\left(v_{n}^{2}(t-t_{n})^{2}+h_{n}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. In case
{\tau}_{n}=1
, both collections of sets
\left\{E^{t,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq t_{1}}
and
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq t_{1}}
, are increasing, since for any
t\geq t_{1}
and the point
(t,\hat{h}_{1}(t))
lies on the increasing wing of the radial bird
C^{1}_{(t_{1},h_{1})}
, see Picture (
11
) of Figure
8.1
. For
{\tau}_{n}=2
, the collection
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq t_{1}}
is increasing, whereas the collection
\left\{E^{t,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq t_{1}}
is non-monotonic, see Picture (
12
) of Figure
8.1
. Indeed, for any
\varepsilon>0
arbitrarily small, and
t\geq t_{n}
, we have
E^{t,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\not\subset E^{t+\varepsilon,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(t+\varepsilon)}\setminus E^{0,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
.

t
\bullet
(t_{1},h_{1})

E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}

1

For
{\tau}_{n}=1
and
t\geq t_{1}
, the regions
E^{t,v_{l}}_{h_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
, for
l=1,2
, are increasing. We just draw two sets of half-ellipses at time
t=\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}_{2}
, where
h_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})=\hat{h}_{1}
and

.

t
\bullet

{\tau}_{n}=2
and

E^{t,v_{2}}_{h_{2}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
are increasing, where as
E^{t,v_{1}}_{h_{2}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
is non-monotonic. We just draw two sets of half-ellipses at time

, where
h_{2}(\hat{s}_{1})=\hat{h}_{1}
and

.

.

or
