8.3.3.
Palm distribution of inter-handover time

The following theorem determines the Laplace transform of
T
, which is equal to
T_{1}(0)
, under the Palm probability distribution
\mathbb{P}^{0}_{\mathcal{V}}
in terms of the head point process
{\mathcal{H}}
. Using this alternative nomenclature for handover types, for pure handovers of type
i
or equivalently

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
i,i\end{array}\bigg]

, we denote the pure handover point processes and their intensities as
{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
and
L_{i}
, for
i=1,2
, respectively. ​​​​​​ Similarly, we denote the mixed handover point processes and their intensities as
{\mathcal{W}}^{(q)}_{i,j}
and
L^{(q)}_{i,j}
, with
{\tau}_{p}=i
and
{\tau}_{n}=j\neq i
, respectively, for mixed handovers of type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
i,j\end{array}\bigg]

. ​​​​​​ We refer to Table
8.1
for the bijection between the two sets of notations. We also write the entire handover point process as

{\mathcal{V}}\equiv{\mathcal{W}}:=\sum_{i\in\{1,2\}}{\mathcal{W}}_{i}+\sum_{i\neq j,q\in\{1,2\}}{\mathcal{W}}^{(q)}_{i,j},

and
\lambda_{{\mathcal{W}}}=\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}
.

Theorem 8.18
.

Let
\rho\geq 0
. In the two-speed case, under the Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}}
of handovers, the Laplace transform of inter-handover time
T
is given by,

(8.38)

\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{W}}}^{0}[e^{-\rho T}]=\sum_{i\in\{1,2\}}\frac{L_{i}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{W}}}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{i}}[e^{-\rho T}]+\sum_{i\neq j,q\in\{1,2\}}\frac{L^{(q)}_{i,j}}{\lambda_{\mathcal{W}}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{i,j}^{(q)}}[e^{-\rho T}].

Proof of Theorem
8.38
.

The proof is based on the application of Theorem
8.2
to the decomposition of the Palm probability measure. To get the result, we apply the decomposition (
8.4
) to the function
f({\mathcal{H}})=e^{-\rho T}
, for any
\rho\geq 0
. The terms
\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{i}}[e^{-\rho T}]
, for
i\in\{1,2\}
, correspond to the individual Palm probability measures for pure handovers of type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
i,i\end{array}\bigg]

and
\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{i,j}^{(q)}}[e^{-\rho T}]
, for
i\neq j,q\in\{1,2\}
, correspond to the individual Palm probability measures for mixed handovers of type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
i,j\end{array}\bigg]

, which are determined in the following lemmas.
∎

Lemma 8.19
.

For any
\rho\geq 0
, under the Palm probability measure of pure handovers of type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
i,i\end{array}\bigg]

with
{\tau}_{p}={\tau}_{n}=i
, the Laplace transform of
T
is

(8.39)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{i}}[e^{-\rho T}]

\displaystyle=\frac{4\lambda_{i}^{2}v_{i}^{2}}{L_{i}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{3}}\zeta_{i,i}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}){\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h,

for
i=1,2
, where the function
\zeta_{1,1}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
is determined in (
8.66
) and
\zeta_{2,2}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, in (
8.82
).

Lemma 8.20
.

For
i\neq j,q\in\{1,2\}
, under the Palm probability measure of mixed handovers of type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
i,j\end{array}\bigg]

, the Laplace transform of
T
is

(8.40)

\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{i,j}^{(q)}}[e^{-\rho T}]=\frac{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}{L^{(q)}_{i,j}}\,\xi_{i,j}^{q}(\rho,v_{i},v_{j}),

where the functions
\xi_{i,j}^{(q)}(\rho,v_{i},v_{j})
are determined in (
8.106
), (
8.122
), (
8.140
) and (
8.168
), respectively, for the four different cases for
i\neq j,q\in\{1,2\}
.

Remark 8.21
.

In the expressions of the mixed Palm probability measures, the functions
\xi_{i,j}^{(q)}(\rho,v_{i},v_{j})
for
i\neq j,q\in\{1,2\}
, are also functions of the underlying parameters
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
. We skip the dependence in
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
for brevity.

The derivation of
\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{i}}[e^{-\rho T}]
with
i\in\{1,2\}
,
{\tau}_{p}={\tau}_{n}=i
, can be found in Subsubsection
8.3.5
for the case

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
1,1\end{array}\bigg]

and Subsubsection
8.3.6
for the case

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
2,2\end{array}\bigg]

. We derive expressions for
\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}^{(q)}_{i,j}}[e^{-\rho T}]
with
i\neq j,q\in\{1,2\}
in Subsubsection
8.3.7
for the case

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
2,1\end{array}\bigg]

, Subsubsection
8.3.8
for

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
1,2\end{array}\bigg]

, Subsubsection
8.3.9
for

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
2,1\end{array}\bigg]

, and Subsubsection
8.3.10
for

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
1,2\end{array}\bigg]

, respectively.

8.3.4.
Handover pair type

As a general principle, we denote by

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q,q^{\prime}\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]

the type of all pairs of consecutive handovers with

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n}\end{array}\bigg]

the type of the first handover,
{\tau}_{u}
the type of the radial bird corresponding to the upcoming station and

(8.41)

q^{\prime}:=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }t_{u}\geq t_{n},\\
2&\text{ if }t_{u}<t_{n}.\end{cases}

In this composite notation

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q,q^{\prime}\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]

of a pair of consecutive handovers,

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q^{\prime}\\
{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]

denotes the type of the next handover. This notation encodes the information about the types and relative positions of three head points involved in two consecutive handovers. Table
8.2
below describes all possible pairs of consecutive handovers, that can be produced from each of the type of handovers.

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q,q^{\prime}\\

, associated to each individual type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\

. The special case:

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q,q^{\prime}\\

, denotes the reappearance phenomenon where the previous bird coincides with the upcoming bird.

This table also summarizes the strategy to sort out the Palm probability distribution of inter-handover time, depending on the type of initial handover and the type of the radial bird producing the next handover. There can be a scenario where the same station of type
{\tau}_{p}
, that was a serving station previously, becomes the upcoming serving station again. We denote the pair type in this situation as

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q,q^{\prime}\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{p}^{*}\end{array}\bigg]

, where we use
\cdot^{*}
to denote this reappearance of the same station corresponding to
{\tau}_{p}
, as the upcoming serving station.

Proofs of Lemma
8.19
and Lemma
8.20
:
In determining the individual Palm expectations in (
8.38
), through Lemma
8.19
and Lemma
8.20
, we frequently use the coordinates of the pure and mixed intersections of the radial birds. Suppose
(t_{1},h_{1}),(t_{2},h_{2})\in\mathbb{H}^{+}
, are two head points of same or different types. From now onward the subscript used in the notation of the head point, does not correspond to it’s type, unlike in Subsubsection
8.3.1
. The intersection point is denoted by
(\hat{s}(t_{1},h_{1},t_{2},h_{2}),\hat{h}(t_{1},h_{1},t_{2},h_{2}))
, when the two birds are of the same type. On the other hand, when the two birds are of different types, the intersection points are denoted by
(\hat{s}_{1}(t_{1},h_{1},t_{2},h_{2}),\hat{h}_{1}(t_{1},h_{1},t_{2},h_{2}))
and
(\hat{s}_{2}(t_{1},h_{1},t_{2},h_{2}),\hat{h}_{2}(t_{1},h_{1},t_{2},h_{2}))
. In the following, we may use slightly different notations, wherever necessary in the proofs.

8.3.5.
Proof of Lemma
8.19
, type
\binom{1}{1,1}
or equivalently

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
1,1\end{array}\bigg]

Proof.

Using Lemma
8.3
for the first term in (
8.38
) with
{\tau}_{n}=1={\tau}_{p}
and
q=1
, we have

(8.42)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{1}}[e^{-\rho T}]

\displaystyle=\frac{4\lambda_{1}^{2}v_{1}^{2}}{L_{1}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{3}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[e^{-\rho(T\circ\theta_{\hat{s}})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h,

where we consider two radial birds of type
{\tau}_{n}=1={\tau}_{p}
at
(t_{n},h_{n})=(0,h)
and
(t_{p},h_{p})=(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
and where, in short, we wrote
(\hat{s}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),\hat{h}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}))=(\hat{s},\hat{h})
. The station corresponding to the head point
(0,h)
is the serving station after time
\hat{s}
, since
{\tau}_{n}=1
and
q=1
. Since
(0,h)
is of type
1
, we define
\hat{h}_{1}(t):=\left(v_{1}^{2}t^{2}+h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
and write
\hat{h}_{1}(t)=\hat{h}_{t}
, within this proof for brevity. Observe that

T\circ\theta_{\hat{s}}=T_{1}(\hat{s})-\hat{s}
, where
T_{1}(\hat{s})
is the time until the first handover after time
\hat{s}
. The inner expectation in (
8.42
) can be evaluated by looking for the next handover
given by the intersection of the radial bird with its head at
(0,h)
and a third radial bird with its head at, say
(T_{j},H_{j})
, in view of Corollary
8.14
, which is either of type 1 or type 2, as depicted in Figure
8.3
.

Case 1.

(T_{j},H_{j})
is of type 1.
In this case
(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}
is outside the region
E^{\hat{s},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}}
. Necessarily,
T_{j}\geq 0
. Indeed, if
T_{j}<0
, then the intersection of the birds with head at
(0,h)
and
(T_{j},H_{j})
, either produces a handover before time
\hat{s}
or lies in
{\mathcal{L}}^{+}_{e}(v_{1},v_{2})
, the open region above the joint lower envelope, defined in (
7.5
). Thus
T_{j}\geq 0
and the type of pair of consecutive handovers is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
1,1,1\end{array}\bigg]

. The upcoming head point
(T_{j},H_{j})
of type
1
can be found in the unexplored region defined as
Q_{0}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}}
. Let
(\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}))
be the unique intersection between the two birds and, in this case,
T_{1}(\hat{s}):=\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})
.
For this intersection to give that the next handover takes place immediately after time
\hat{s}
, the region
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s},\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
, beyond
E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}
, must have no point of
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
for
l=1,2
. The required region is constructed as a union, using the increasing sets as in Lemma
8.12
and the Definition
8.13
, due to the potential lack of monotonicity among the individual sets in the union.
This corresponds to the first term in (
8.43
) and is also depicted in Picture (
31
) of Figure
8.3
.

Case 2.

(T_{j},H_{j})
is of type 2.
In this case
(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}
, is outside
E^{\hat{s},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}}
and
T_{j}
can be positive or negative. In this case, the unexplored region is
\mathbb{H}^{+}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}}
for the upcoming head point of type
2
. Then by part (
i
) of Lemma
7.12
, applied to
(s,u)=(\hat{s},\hat{h})
, it is true that the radial birds with head points
(T_{j},H_{j})
and
(0,h)
intersect each other, even though the point
(T_{j},H_{j})
is far on the left or right of
(0,h)
. The type of the pair of handovers is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,2\\
1,1,2\end{array}\bigg]

or

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
1,1,2\end{array}\bigg]

, respectively, depending on whether
T_{j}<0
or
T_{j}\geq 0
.
Let
(\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}))
and
(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}))
, be the two intersection points, as illustrated in Picture (
32
) of Figure
8.3
. By part (
ii
) of Lemma
7.12
, we have
\hat{s}_{1}\leq\hat{s}\leq\hat{s}_{2}
and, by Observation
7.11
, we also have
\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})\leq 0\leq\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})
. The first intersection point cannot correspond to a future handover, since
\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})\leq\hat{s}
, see Picture (
32
) of Figure
8.3
.
The only possibility for a future handover is hence
(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}))
, and in this case,
T_{1}(\hat{s}):=\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})
. Observe that, using Lemma
8.12
, for this intersection to be the next handover, we must have the extra region
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s},\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
empty of points of
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
, for
l=1,2
. The union is due to the Definition
8.13
, and therein, a potential lack of monotonicity among the individual sets in the union.
We have used this in the second and third terms in (
8.43
).

Based on the last discussions on
8.3.5
and
8.3.5
, and with the help of Corollary
8.14
, we can decompose the inner expectation in (
8.42
) as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[e^{-\rho(T_{1}(\hat{s})-\hat{s})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)=0}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{\exists(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq 0,\,\bigcap_{l=1,2}\left\{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s},\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}=0\right\}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)=0}\right]{}

\displaystyle\;+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{\exists(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}<0,\,\bigcap_{l=1,2}\left\{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s},\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}=0\right\}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)=0}\right]{}

(8.43)

\displaystyle\;+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{\exists(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq 0,\,\bigcap_{l=1,2}\left\{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s},\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}=0\right\}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)=0}\right].

Using the fact that there is a unique point satisfying the condition in each case, the last expression can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq 0}\;\;\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s},\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)=0}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s})}\right]

\displaystyle\;\;+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}<0}\;\;\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s},\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)=0}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s})}\right]{}

(8.44)

\displaystyle\;\;+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq 0}\;\;\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s},\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)=0}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s})}\right].

For the intersections
(\hat{s},\hat{h})
and
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
to represent two consecutive handovers, the regions
E^{\hat{s},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
and
E^{\hat{s},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
must have no points from
{\mathcal{H}}^{1}
and
{\mathcal{H}}^{2}
, respectively. Here
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
is a realization of
(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}))
.

Figure 8.3
.
The two cases in (
8.44
), i.e., the next handover can be given by a bird at
(T_{j},H_{j})=(t_{1},h_{1})
of type
1
or
2
, with

lying outside

, respectively.

Using part (

, the increasing property of the sequence of regions
\left\{E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right\}_{t\geq\hat{s}}
, we have, for both
l=1,2

\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s},\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}\!\!E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}=E^{\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\text{ and}\!\!\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s},\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}\!\!E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}=E^{\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}},

depending on whether

is of type

or type

. Hence, (

) can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq 0}\;\;\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)=0}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s})}\right]

\displaystyle\;\;+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}<0}\;\;\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)=0}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s})}\right]{}

\displaystyle\;\;+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq 0}\;\;\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)=0}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s})}\right]{}

\displaystyle:=\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
1,1,1\end{array}\bigg]$}+\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,2\\
1,1,2\end{array}\bigg]$}+\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
1,1,2\end{array}\bigg]$}.

We formally define the notation
\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q,q^{\prime}\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]$}
, adopting here a general convention to be used for the rest of the article. Suppose the typical handover is given by the head points
(t_{p},h_{p}),(t_{n},h_{n})
. Then
\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q,q^{\prime}\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]$}
is a function of
\rho,h_{n},|t_{p}-t_{n}|,h_{p}
, i.e.,
\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q,q^{\prime}\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]$}\equiv\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q,q^{\prime}\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]$}(\rho,h_{n},|t_{p}-t_{n}|,h_{p})
. The function
\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q,q^{\prime}\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]$}(\rho,h_{n},|t_{p}-t_{n}|,h_{n})
is the conditional Laplace transform of the inter-handover time
T
and the event that the upcoming handover is of type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q^{\prime}\\
{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]

, given that the typical handover type is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n}\end{array}\bigg]

, with previous head at
(t_{p},h_{p})
and next at
(t_{n},h_{n})
. For
\rho=0
,
\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q,q^{\prime}\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]$}(0,h_{n},|t_{p}-t_{n}|,h_{p})
is the conditional probability of the event that the head point corresponding to the upcoming station is of type
{\tau}_{u}
and the corresponding head point is on the right or left side of
(t_{n},h_{n})
depending on whether
q^{\prime}=1
or
2
. In the current proof, since
\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n}\end{array}\bigg]$}=\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
1,1\end{array}\bigg]$}
, we have
\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,q^{\prime}\\
1,1,{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]$}
as functions of

, for different values of
{\tau}_{u}
and

, as appeared in (

), for which we evaluate the individual terms.

Applying the Campbell-Mecke formula, the first term in (

) equals

\displaystyle\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\hat{s}+\hat{h}/v_{1}}\int_{\left(\hat{h}^{2}-v_{1}^{2}(t_{1}-\hat{s})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}h_{1}\,{\rm d}t_{1}{}

(8.53)

\displaystyle\;\;+2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\int_{\hat{s}+\hat{h}/v_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}h_{1}\,{\rm d}t_{1},

\displaystyle\;\;+2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\int_{\hat{s}-\hat{h}/v_{2}}^{\hat{s}+\hat{h}/v_{2}}\int_{\left(\hat{h}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}(t_{1}-\hat{s})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\infty}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}-\hat{s})}e^{-\sum_{l=1,2}2\lambda_{l}v_{l}\left|E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right|}{\rm d}h_{1}\,{\rm d}t_{1}{}

\displaystyle\;\;+2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\int_{\hat{s}+\hat{h}/v_{2}}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}-\hat{s})}e^{-\sum_{l=1,2}2\lambda_{l}v_{l}\left|E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right|}{\rm d}h_{1}\,{\rm d}t_{1}{}

(8.66)

\displaystyle:=\zeta_{1,1}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}).

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{1}}[e^{-\rho T}]

\displaystyle=\frac{4\lambda_{1}^{2}v_{1}^{2}}{L_{1}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{3}}\zeta_{1,1}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}){\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h.

.

8.3.6.
Proof of Lemma

, type
\binom{1}{2,2}
or equivalently

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\

Proof.

Using Lemma
8.3
for the second term in (

), with

and

, we have

(8.68)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{2}}[e^{-\rho T}]

\displaystyle=\frac{4\lambda_{2}^{2}v_{2}^{2}}{L_{2}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{3}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[e^{-\rho(T_{1}\circ\theta_{\hat{s}})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h,

where we consider two radial birds of type
{\tau}_{n}=2={\tau}_{p}
at

and
(t_{p},h_{p})=(-t^{\prime},h)
and where, in short, we wrote

. Note that, since
{\tau}_{n}=2

, the station corresponding to the head point

\hat{s}
. Since
(0,h)

, which we write in short as

within this proof. Notice that

where

is the time to the handover after time
\hat{s}
. By Corollary

, the inner expectation in (

Case 1.

. For any of these points to represent a handover we need the extra regions
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s},\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
and
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s},\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
to be empty of points from

8.3.7.
Proof of Lemma
8.20
, type
\binom{1}{2,1}
or equivalently

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
2,1\end{array}\bigg]

Proof.

In this case we have
{\tau}_{n}=1,{\tau}_{p}=2
,
q=1
.
Using Lemma
8.4
, for the mixed Palm expectations in (
8.38
) with these values of
{\tau}_{n},{\tau}_{p},q
and
f({\mathcal{H}})=e^{-\rho T}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{L^{(1)}_{2,1}}{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{2,1}^{(1)}}[e^{-\rho T}]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[e^{-\rho(T\circ\theta_{\hat{s}_{1}})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

(8.83)

\displaystyle\;+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[e^{-\rho(T\circ\theta_{\hat{s}_{1}})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h,

where
(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{h}_{1})=(\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),\hat{h}_{1}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}))
denotes the coordinates of the first intersection between the radial birds at
(t_{n},h_{n})=(0,h)
of type
{\tau}_{n}=1
and
(t_{p},h_{p})=(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
type
{\tau}_{p}=2
, which are responsible for a handover. The coordinates
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})=(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}))
denote the second intersection. Since
{\tau}_{n}=1
and
q=1
, the station corresponding to the head point
(0,h)
becomes the serving station after time
\hat{s}_{1}
. The head point
(0,h)
being of type
1
, we define
\hat{h}_{1}(t):=\left(v_{1}^{2}t^{2}+h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
, which we write in short as
\hat{h}_{t}
locally. Observe that

T\circ\theta_{\hat{s}_{1}}=T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})-\hat{s}_{1},

where
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})
is the time of the next handover after time
\hat{s}_{1}
. For the next handover, the extra region
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
beyond
E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
must have no points of
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
, for
l=1,2
. Here we have for any
\hat{s}\geq\hat{s}_{2}

\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}_{2}]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\subset\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}.

Hence, the next handover can happen before or at
\hat{s}_{2}
, i.e.,
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})<\hat{s}_{2}
or
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})=\hat{s}_{2}
, which leads to another layer of decomposition.

Case 1.

T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})<\hat{s}_{2}
.

By Corollary
8.14
, the inner expectation in both terms in (
8.83
) can be evaluated by looking for the next handover created by a radial bird with head at, say
(T_{j},H_{j})
, which is of type 1 or type 2. The head point
(T_{j},H_{j})
must be located outside
E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
and
E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
, depending on its type
1
or
2
(see Figure
8.7
), as follows:

Subcase 1.1.

(T_{j},H_{j})
is of type 1.
Here, it must be the case that
T_{j}\geq 0
and
(T_{j},H_{j})\notin E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
. Indeed, since, for any radial bird with the head point at
(T_{j},H_{j})\notin E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
, such that
T_{j}<0
, the intersection to the radial bird with head at
(0,h)
, does not contribute to a future handover as it intersects the one with head at
(0,h)
, before time
\hat{s}_{1}
. Thus the unexplored region must be
Q_{0}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
and the type of the pair of handovers is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
2,1,1\end{array}\bigg]

.
In this case, the handover is given by the intersection
(\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}))
, as both
(0,h)
and
(T_{j},H_{j})
are of the same type, see Picture (
71
) of Figure
8.7
. Thus we have
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1}):=\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})
. For this intersection to give the next handover immediately after time
\hat{s}_{1}
, the extra region
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
, beyond
E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
, must have no point of
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
for
l=1,2
, where
\hat{h}_{t}=(v_{1}^{2}t^{2}+h^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
. The union is due to Definition
8.13
and to the fact that the sets in the union potentially lack monotonicity.

Subcase 1.2.

(T_{j},H_{j})
is of type 2.
Since
(T_{j},H_{j})\notin E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
, the pair of points
(0,h),(T_{j},H_{j})
naturally satisfies the intersection criterion, by part (
i
) of Lemma
7.12
. For
(T_{j},H_{j})
to be an eligible candidate, we must have
T_{j}\geq-t^{\prime}
. Otherwise if
T_{j}<-t^{\prime}
, then the radial bird with head point at
(T_{j},H_{j})
intersects the one with head point at
(0,h)
, at a point contained in the open region above the lower envelope, as a result this does not contribute to any handover. Hence we assume that
T_{j}\geq-t^{\prime}
and the unexplored region is
Q_{-t^{\prime}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
. Depending on whether
-t^{\prime}\leq T_{j}<0
or
T_{j}\geq 0
, the type of the pair of consecutive handovers is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,2\\
2,1,2\end{array}\bigg]

or

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
2,1,2\end{array}\bigg]

, respectively.

In both cases,
-t^{\prime}\leq T_{j}<0
or
T_{j}\geq 0
, there are two intersections
(\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}))
and
(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}))
between the radial birds at
(0,h)
and
(T_{j},H_{j})
. The first intersection
(\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}))
can never correspond to a handover, because it is contained in
{\mathcal{L}}^{+}_{e}(v_{1},v_{2})
. So the only intersection that can possibly be responsible for a handover is
(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}))
, as described in Figure
8.8
. Hence, we have,
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1}):=\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})
.
For this intersection to give the next handover after time
\hat{s}_{1}
, the region
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
, beyond
E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
, must have no point of
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
for
l=1,2
, where
\hat{h}_{t}=(v_{1}^{2}t^{2}+h^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
. The union is due to Definition
8.13
and the individual sets in the union possibly lack monotonicity.

Case 2.

T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})=\hat{s}_{2}
.
Then the next handover is given by the second intersection
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
of the radial birds with head points
(0,h)
,
(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, provided the region
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}_{2}]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
has no points of
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
, for
l=1,2
, see Figure
8.8
, where the union is due to Definition
8.13
and to the fact that the individual sets therein may not be monotonic. The type of the pair of consecutive handover is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,2\\
2,1,2^{*}\end{array}\bigg]

.

Thus, based on the last arguments, we write the inner expectation in (

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[e^{-\rho(T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})-\hat{s}_{1})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[\mathbbmss{1}_{\exists(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq 0,\,\bigcap_{l=1,2}\left\{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}=0\right\}}\mathbbmss{1}_{\hat{s}_{1}\leq\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})\leq\hat{s}_{2}}{}

\displaystyle\hskip 274.6262pt\times e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{1})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\Bigg]{}

\displaystyle\;\;+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[\mathbbmss{1}_{\exists(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq-t^{\prime},\,\bigcap_{l=1,2}\left\{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}=0\right\}}\mathbbmss{1}_{\hat{s}_{1}\leq\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})\leq\hat{s}_{2}}{}

\displaystyle\hskip 274.6262pt\times e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{1})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\Bigg]{}

(8.84)

\displaystyle\;\;+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}-\hat{s}_{1})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{\{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}_{2}]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}=0\}}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\right].

Similarly to (
8.44
), there exists a unique head point that satisfies the condition in each term of the sum in (
8.84
), the last expression can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq 0}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\mathbbmss{1}_{\hat{s}_{1}\leq\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})\leq\hat{s}_{2}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{1})}\right]

\displaystyle\;\;+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq-t^{\prime}}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\mathbbmss{1}_{\hat{s}_{1}\leq\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})\leq\hat{s}_{2}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{1})}\right]{}

(8.85)

\displaystyle\;\;+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}-\hat{s}_{1})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}_{2}]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\right].

Using the increasing property of the sequence of regions
\left\{E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right\}_{t\geq\hat{s}}
from part (
i
) of Proposition
8.16
, in all the terms in (
8.85
), we get

\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}=E^{\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}},

\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}=E^{\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\text{ and }\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}_{2}]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}=E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}},

for

, where the first two equalities depend on whether

is of type
1
or

and the last one is for

and
\hat{s}_{2}
to be consecutive handovers. Thus the three terms in (
8.85
) can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq 0}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\mathbbmss{1}_{\hat{s}_{1}\leq\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})\leq\hat{s}_{2}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{1})}\right]

\displaystyle\;\;+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq-t^{\prime}}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\mathbbmss{1}_{\hat{s}_{1}\leq\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})\leq\hat{s}_{2}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{1})}\right]{}

\displaystyle\;\;+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}-\hat{s}_{1})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\right]:=\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
2,1,1\end{array}\bigg]$}+\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
2,1,2\end{array}\bigg]$}+\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,2\\
2,1,2^{*}\end{array}\bigg]$},

where the type of the pair of handovers marked with
\cdot^{*}
means that we have reappearance of the previous station as an upcoming one, as seen in the last column of Table
8.2
.

Let
R_{1}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\subset(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{2}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}}
be the region defined as

R_{1}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}):=\left\{(t,u):\hat{s}_{1}\leq\hat{s}(0,h,t,u)\leq\hat{s}_{2}\right\},

. It can be proved, using Lemma

) equals

\displaystyle\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
2,1,1\end{array}\bigg]$}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\int_{E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{1})}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,t_{1},t_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}t_{1}{\rm d}h_{1}{}

(8.94)

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\int_{E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{1})}e^{-\sum_{l=1,2}2\lambda_{l}v_{l}\left|E^{\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,t_{1},t_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right|}{\rm d}t_{1}{\rm d}h_{1}.

. It can be proved using Lemma

\displaystyle\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
2,1,2\end{array}\bigg]$}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\int_{E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{1})}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,t_{1},t_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}t_{1}{\rm d}h_{1}{}

(8.97)

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\int_{E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{1})}e^{-\sum_{l=1,2}2\lambda_{l}v_{l}\left|E^{\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,t_{1},t_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right|}{\rm d}t_{1}{\rm d}h_{1}.

) equals

\displaystyle\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,2\\
2,1,2^{*}\end{array}\bigg]$}

\displaystyle=e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}-\hat{s}_{1})}e^{-\sum_{l=1,2}2\lambda_{l}v_{l}\left|E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right|}.

Define

\displaystyle\zeta^{(1)}_{2,1}(\rho,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
2,1,1\end{array}\bigg]$}+\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\

2,1,2^{*}\end{array}\bigg]$},

using (

)–(

). We obtain the result by substituting the expression of

from (

) back to both terms in (

), as

\displaystyle\frac{L^{(1)}_{2,1}}{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{2,1}^{(1)}}[e^{-\rho T}]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}\zeta^{(1)}_{2,1}(\rho,t^{\prime},h^{\prime},h){\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\zeta^{(1)}_{2,1}(\rho,t^{\prime},h^{\prime},h){\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

(8.106)

\displaystyle:=\xi_{2,1}^{(1)}(\rho,v_{2},v_{1}),

which is a function of
\rho,v_{2},v_{1}
only.
∎

8.3.8.
Proof of Lemma
8.20
, type
\binom{2}{2,1}
or equivalently

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
1,2\end{array}\bigg]

Proof.

Using Lemma
8.4
, the mixed Palm expectations in (
8.38
) with
{\tau}_{n}=2,{\tau}_{p}=1
,
q=2
, and
f({\mathcal{H}})=e^{-\rho T}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{L^{(2)}_{1,2}}{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{1,2}^{(2)}}[e^{-\rho T}]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[e^{-\rho(T\circ\theta_{\hat{s}_{2}})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

(8.107)

\displaystyle\;+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[e^{-\rho(T\circ\theta_{\hat{s}_{2}})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h,

where there are two radial birds of type
{\tau}_{n}=2
and
{\tau}_{p}=1
, respectively, with head at
(t_{p},h_{p})=(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
and
(t_{n},h_{n})=(0,h)
and we write in short
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
for
(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}))
to denote the second intersection, see Figure
8.9
. Note that, since
{\tau}_{n}=2
and
q=2
, the station corresponding to the head point
(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
is the serving station after time
\hat{s}_{2}
. Since
(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
is of type
2
, we define
\hat{h}_{t}:=\left(v_{2}^{2}(t+t^{\prime})^{2}+h^{\prime 2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Also note that

T\circ\theta_{\hat{s}_{2}}:=T_{1}(\hat{s}_{2})-\hat{s}_{2},

where
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{2})
is the time point of the next handover after time
\hat{s}_{2}
. The inner expectation in both terms in (
8.107
) can be evaluated by looking for the next handover created by a radial bird with its head at
(T_{j},H_{j})
(say), such that it must lie outside
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
or
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
, depending on its type.

Case 1.

(T_{j},H_{j})
is of type 1.
We must have
T_{j}\geq\hat{s}_{2}
. Otherwise, if
T_{j}<\hat{s}_{2}
, then the intersection produced by the radial bird with head at
(T_{j},H_{j})
, with the one at
(0,h)
, is either contained in
{\mathcal{L}}^{+}_{e}(v_{1},v_{2})
or it is responsible for a handover before the time
\hat{s}_{2}
. Thus
T_{j}\geq\hat{s}_{2}
and the unexplored region is
Q_{\hat{s}_{2}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
. The type of the pair of consecutive handover is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
1,2,1\end{array}\bigg]

. Also
(T_{j},H_{j})
must be such that,
T_{j}\in D_{r}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},H_{j})
, given
t^{\prime},h^{\prime},H_{j}
, for
(T_{j},H_{j})
to be in this case. Suppose
(\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}))
and
(\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}))
be the two intersection point of the radial birds with head at
(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),(T_{j},H_{j})
, such that
\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})\leq\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})
. Observe that, the point
(T_{j},H_{j})
being outside
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
, we have
\hat{s}_{2}\leq\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})
, see Picture (
91
) of Figure
8.9
. For the two intersection points to represent handovers, we must have no point of
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
, for
l=1,2
, in the extra regions
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
and
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
, respectively, beyond
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
, where
\hat{h}_{t}=(v_{2}^{2}(t+t^{\prime})^{2}+h^{\prime 2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Since
\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})\leq\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})
, we have

\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\subset\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}.

Hence, the first intersection
(\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}))
gives the next handover. Thus we have
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1}):=\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})
.

Case 2.

(T_{j},H_{j})
is of type 2.
In this case, the point must be such that
T_{j}\geq-t^{\prime}
. Otherwise, the birds with head at
(T_{j},H_{j})
and
(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, intersects before time
\hat{s}_{2}
, which is not of interest here, since we are looking a future handover after
\hat{s}_{2}
. Thus the unexplored region is
Q_{-t^{\prime}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
and the type of the pair of consecutive handover is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
1,2,2\end{array}\bigg]

. The handover must be given by
(\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}))
, the only intersection between the birds at
(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
and
(T_{j},H_{j})
, see Picture (
92
) of Figure
8.9
. Additionally, we have
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1}):=\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})
. The union is due to the Definition
8.13
and the individual sets in the union may not be monotonic.

t
\bullet
(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
\bullet
\bullet
(t_{1},h_{1})
(0,h)
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})

For the intersection
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
and

to represent two consecutive handovers, the regions

and

must have no points from
{\mathcal{H}}^{1}
and
{\mathcal{H}}^{2}
, respectively. Here

is a realization of
(\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}))
.

t

For the intersection
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
and

to represent two consecutive handovers, the regions

and

must have no points from
{\mathcal{H}}^{1}
and
{\mathcal{H}}^{2}
, respectively. Here
(\tilde{s},\tilde{h})
is a realization of
(\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}))
.

Figure 8.9
.
The two pictures correspond to the two cases in (
8.108
), where the head point
(T_{j},H_{j})=(t_{1},h_{1})
is of type
1
or

.

The last discussion leads to a decomposition of the inner expectation in (
8.107
) as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[e^{-\rho(T_{1}(\hat{s}_{2})-\hat{s}_{2})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{\exists(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq\hat{s}_{2},\,T_{j}\in D_{r}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},H_{j}),\,\bigcap_{l=1,2}\left\{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}=0\right\}}\right.{}

\displaystyle\hskip 267.39877pt\times\left.e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{2})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\right]{}

\displaystyle\;\;+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{\exists(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,\bigcap_{l=1,2}\left\{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}=0\right\}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{2})}\right.{}

(8.108)

\displaystyle\hskip 267.39877pt\times\left.\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\right]

Since there exists a unique head point satisfying the condition in the last sum, it reduces to

\displaystyle\hskip-43.36243pt\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq\hat{s}_{2},T_{j}\in D_{r}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},H_{j})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{2})}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{2})}\right]{}

\displaystyle\hskip-43.36243pt:=\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
1,2,1\end{array}\bigg]$}+\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
1,2,2\end{array}\bigg]$}.

For the first term in (
8.3.8
), using Remark
7.10
for
l=1
, we have

\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}=E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\cup S_{1}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})},

where
\hat{h}_{t}=(v_{2}^{2}(t+t^{\prime})^{2}+h^{\prime 2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
S_{1}
is the region outside
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})}
but below the hyperbola given by the equation
u^{2}-\frac{v_{1}^{2}v_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}(t+t^{\prime})^{2}=h^{\prime 2}
, in the
(t,u)
-coordinate system similar to (
7.17
) and within the time interval

\left[\frac{1}{v_{1}^{2}}\left((v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2})\hat{s}_{2}-v_{2}^{2}t^{\prime}\right),\frac{1}{v_{1}^{2}}\left((v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2})\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})-v_{2}^{2}t^{\prime}\right)\right].

For
l=2
, using the increasing property of
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}}\right\}_{t\geq\hat{s}_{2}}
, from part (
ii
) of Proposition
8.16
, we have

\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}}=E^{\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}}.

Then the first term in (
8.3.8
) equals

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq\hat{s}_{2},T_{j}\in D_{r}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},H_{j})}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})}\cup S_{1}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\right.

(8.113)

\displaystyle\hskip 158.99377pt\times\left.\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{2})}\right].

The point
(T_{j},H_{j})
must lie in the region
R_{1}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, where

(8.114)

R_{1}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}):=\left\{(t,u)\,\mbox{:}\,t\geq\hat{s}_{2},\,v_{1}^{2}(t-\hat{s}_{2})^{2}+u^{2}\geq\hat{h}_{2}^{2}\;\text{ and }\;u^{2}-\frac{v_{1}^{2}v_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}(t+t^{\prime})^{2}\leq h^{\prime 2}\right\}.

For the region
R_{1}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
in (
8.114
), the first inequality
t\geq\hat{s}_{2}
is due to the fact that the head point should lie on the right of
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
, as discussed in
8.3.8
of this proof. The second and the third condition are because the next head point must lie outside
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
and below the hyperbola
u^{2}-\frac{v_{1}^{2}v_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}(t+t^{\prime})^{2}=h^{\prime 2}
, by Property
II
. The latter is equivalent to the intersection criteria of two birds.

Applying the Campbell-Mecke formula, the first term in (
8.3.8
) equals

\displaystyle\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
1,2,1\end{array}\bigg]$}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\int_{R_{1}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{2})}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})}\cup S_{1}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\right.{}

\displaystyle\hskip 216.81pt\times\left.\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}t_{1}{\rm d}h_{1}{}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\int_{\hat{s}_{2}}^{\hat{s}_{2}+\hat{h}_{2}/v_{1}}\int_{\left(\hat{h}_{2}^{2}-v_{1}^{2}(t_{1}-\hat{s}_{2})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\left(\frac{v_{1}^{2}v_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}(t_{1}+t^{\prime})^{2}+h^{\prime 2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{2})}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})}\cup S_{1}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\right.{}

\displaystyle\hskip 216.81pt\times\left.\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}h_{1}{\rm d}t_{1}{}

\displaystyle\;\;+2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\int_{\hat{s}_{2}+\hat{h}_{2}/v_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\left(\frac{v_{1}^{2}v_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}(t_{1}+t^{\prime})^{2}+h^{\prime 2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{2})}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\left(S_{1}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\right.{}

(8.117)

\displaystyle\hskip 216.81pt\times\left.\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}h_{1}{\rm d}t_{1}.

For the second term in (
8.3.8
), using Remark
7.10
for
l=1
,

\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}=E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\cup S_{2}\cup E^{\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})},

where
\hat{h}_{t}=(v_{2}^{2}(t+t^{\prime})^{2}+h^{\prime 2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
and

is the region outside
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\cup E^{\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})}
but below the hyperbola given by the equation

, in the

-coordinate system similar to (

For
l=2
, using part (
ii
) of Proposition
8.16
, the increasing property of
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}}\right\}_{t\geq\hat{s}_{2}}
, we have

\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}}=E^{\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}}.

The second term in (
8.3.8
) equals

\displaystyle\hskip-238.49121pt\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\cup S_{2}\cup E^{\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})}\right)=0}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\cup E^{\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})}\right)=0}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{2})}\right].

The point
(T_{j},H_{j})
must lie in the region
R_{2}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, where

(8.118)

R_{2}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}):=\left\{(t,u):t\geq-t^{\prime},u\geq 0,\text{ and }v_{2}^{2}(t-\hat{s}_{2})^{2}+u^{2}\geq\hat{h}_{2}^{2}\right\}.

For the region
R_{2}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
in (
8.118
), the first condition

, is as discussed in
8.3.8
of the proof. The last condition is due to the fact that the next head point must lie outside

.

Applying the Campbell-Mecke formula and later evaluating void probabilities, the second term in (
8.3.8
) equals

\displaystyle\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
1,2,2\end{array}\bigg]$}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\int_{R_{2}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{2})}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\left(E^{\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})}\cup S_{2}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\right.{}

\displaystyle\hskip 216.81pt\times\left.\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\left(E^{\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}t_{1}{\rm d}h_{1}{}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\int_{-t^{\prime}}^{\hat{s}_{2}}\int_{\left(\hat{h}_{2}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}(t_{1}-\hat{s}_{2})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\infty}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{2})}e^{-2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\left|E^{\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})}\cup S_{2}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{}

\displaystyle\hskip 252.94499pt\times e^{-2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\left|E^{\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{\rm d}t_{1}{\rm d}h_{1}{}

\displaystyle\;\;+2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\int_{\hat{s}_{2}}^{\hat{s}_{2}+\hat{h}_{2}/v_{2}}\int_{\left(\hat{h}_{2}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}(t_{1}-\hat{s}_{2})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\infty}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{2})}e^{-2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\left|E^{\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})}\cup S_{2}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{}

\displaystyle\hskip 252.94499pt\times e^{-2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\left|E^{\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{\rm d}t_{1}{\rm d}h_{1}{}

\displaystyle\;\;+2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\int_{\hat{s}_{2}+\hat{h}_{2}/v_{2}}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{2})}e^{-2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\left|E^{\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})}\cup S_{2}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{}

(8.121)

\displaystyle\hskip 252.94499pt\times e^{-2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\left|E^{\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{\rm d}t_{1}{\rm d}h_{1}.

Let us denote the sum of all the terms in (
8.117
) and (
8.121
) as
\zeta^{(2)}_{1,2}(\rho,t^{\prime},h^{\prime},h)=\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
1,2,1\end{array}\bigg]$}+\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
1,2,2\end{array}\bigg]$}
. Using the expression for
\zeta^{(2)}_{1,2}(\rho,t^{\prime},h^{\prime},h)
back in (
8.107
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{L^{(2)}_{1,2}}{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{1,2}^{(2)}}[e^{-\rho T}]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}\zeta^{(2)}_{1,2}(\rho,t^{\prime},h^{\prime},h){\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\zeta^{(2)}_{1,2}(\rho,t^{\prime},h^{\prime},h){\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

(8.122)

\displaystyle:=\xi_{1,2}^{(2)}(\rho,v_{2},v_{1}),

which is a function of
\rho,v_{2},v_{1}
only.
∎

8.3.9.
Proof of Lemma
8.20
, type
\binom{1}{1,2}
or equivalently

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
2,1\end{array}\bigg]

Proof.

Using Lemma
8.4
, for the mixed Palm expectations in (
8.38
) with
{\tau}_{n}=2,{\tau}_{p}=1
,
q=2
and
f({\mathcal{H}})=e^{-\rho T}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{L^{(2)}_{2,1}}{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{2,1}^{(2)}}[e^{-\rho T}]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[e^{-\rho(T\circ\theta_{\hat{s}_{1}})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

(8.123)

\displaystyle\;+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[e^{-\rho(T\circ\theta_{\hat{s}_{1}})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h.

where
(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{h}_{1})=(\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),\hat{h}_{1}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}))
is the first intersection of the birds at
(t_{p},h_{p})=(0,h)
and
(t_{n},h_{n})=(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, which are of type
{\tau}_{p}=2
and
{\tau}_{n}=1
, respectively.

The station corresponding to the head point
(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
is the serving station after time
\hat{s}_{1}
. Since
(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
is of type
1
, we define
\hat{h}_{1}(t):=\left(v_{1}^{2}(t+t^{\prime})^{2}+h^{\prime 2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
, which write in short as
\hat{h}_{t}
. Observe that

T\circ\theta_{\hat{s}_{1}}=T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})-\hat{s}_{1},

where
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})
is the time point of the first handover after time
\hat{s}_{1}
. The intersection point
(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{h}_{1})
corresponding to the typical handover in this case, the second intersection
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
will correspond to the next handover, if there is no radial bird that intersects the bird at
(0,h)
before time
\hat{s}_{2}
, i.e.,
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})<\hat{s}_{2}
or the next handover happens at time
\hat{s}_{2}
, in which case
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})=\hat{s}_{2}
. The reason for the next handover to happen at most before
\hat{s}_{2}
, will be clear later in the following cases.

Case 1.

T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})<\hat{s}_{2}
.
The next handover can be given by a bird with head point, say
(T_{j},H_{j})
, outside the region
E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
or
E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
, depending on its type. Thus we have two sub-cases:

Subcase 1.1.

(T_{j},H_{j})
is of type 1.
In this case,
(T_{j},H_{j})
must be at least on the right of
(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, i.e.,
T_{j}\geq-t^{\prime}
. Otherwise, the next radial bird with head point
(T_{j},H_{j})
being outside
E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
and
T_{j}<-t^{\prime}
, intersects the one with head at
(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, at a point that is either contained in
{\mathcal{L}}^{+}_{e}(v_{1},v_{2})
or contributes to a handover before time
\hat{s}_{1}
, which is not of interest. Then, we must have
T_{j}\geq-t^{\prime}
and the unexplored region is
Q_{-t^{\prime}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
. The type of the pair of consecutive handovers is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
2,1,1\end{array}\bigg]

.
There is just one intersection, say
(\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}))
between the radial birds at
(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
and
(T_{j},H_{j})
and handover is given by that, see Picture (
101
) of Figure
8.10
. Thus we have
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1}):=\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})
. In this case, the extra region
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
must have no points of
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
for
l=1,2
. The union is due to Definition
8.13
and to the fact that the individual sets in the union may lack monotonicity.

Subcase 1.2.

(T_{j},H_{j})
is of type 2.
Here it must be the case that
T_{j}\geq 0
. Otherwise, if
T_{j}<0
, then the two birds with head point
(T_{j},H_{j})
and
(0,h)
intersect at a point before time
\hat{s}_{1}
, which is not relevant in this case. Then, we must have
T_{j}\geq 0\geq-t^{\prime}
and the unexplored region is
Q_{0}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
. The type of the pair of consecutive handovers is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
2,1,2\end{array}\bigg]

. By Lemma
7.12
, there are two intersections
(\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}))
and
(\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j}))
between the birds at
(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),(T_{j},H_{j})
. The first intersection cannot give rise to a handover, as it is contained in
{\mathcal{L}}^{+}_{e}(v_{1},v_{2})
. So the next handover must be given by the second intersection, see Picture (
102
) of Figure
8.10
. Thus we have
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1}):=\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})
. The last intersection to give a handover, the extra region
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
must have not points of
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
for
l=1,2
. The union is due to Definition
8.13
and the fact that individual sets in the union potentially lack monotonicity.

Based on last discussions we have the following decomposition of the inner expectation in (

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[e^{-\rho(T_{1}(\hat{s}_{1})-\hat{s}_{1})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[\mathbbmss{1}_{\exists(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq-t^{\prime},\,\bigcap_{l=1,2}\{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}=0\}}\mathbbmss{1}_{\hat{s}_{1}\leq\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})\leq\hat{s}_{2}}{}

\displaystyle\;\;+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[\mathbbmss{1}_{\exists(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq 0,\,\bigcap_{l=1,2}\{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}=0\}}\mathbbmss{1}_{\hat{s}_{1}\leq\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})\leq\hat{s}_{2}}{}

\displaystyle\hskip 245.71754pt\times e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{1})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\Bigg]{}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}-\hat{s}_{1})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}_{2}]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}=0}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\right]{}

\displaystyle:=\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\

\displaystyle\hskip 289.07999pt\times e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{1})}\Bigg]{}

R_{2}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})=E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}
.
Using this along with the application of the Campbell-Mecke formula, we get that the second term in (
8.130
) equals

\displaystyle\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
2,1,2\end{array}\bigg]$}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\int_{E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{1})}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},t_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}t_{1}{\rm d}h_{1}.

Using void probabilities in (
8.3.9
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
2,1,2\end{array}\bigg]$}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\int_{E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{1})}e^{-\sum_{l=1,2}2\lambda_{l}v_{l}\left|E^{\hat{s}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},h_{1}),v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},t_{1},t_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right|}{\rm d}t_{1}{\rm d}h_{1}.

For the third term in (
8.130
), we have

\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{1},\hat{s}_{2}]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}=E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}},

and hence this third term equals

\displaystyle\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
2,1,2^{*}\end{array}\bigg]$}

\displaystyle=e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{2}-\hat{s}_{1})}e^{-\sum_{l=1,2}2\lambda_{l}v_{l}\left|E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{1},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{1}}\right|}.

Let us denote the sum of all the terms in (
8.133
)–(
8.3.9
) as
\zeta^{(2)}_{2,1}(\rho,t^{\prime},h^{\prime},h)=\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
2,1,1\end{array}\bigg]$}+\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
2,1,2\end{array}\bigg]$}+\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2,1\\
2,1,2^{*}\end{array}\bigg]$}
. Using the expression for
\zeta^{(2)}_{2,1}(\rho,t^{\prime},h^{\prime},h)
in (
8.123
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{L^{(2)}_{2,1}}{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{2,1}^{(2)}}[e^{-\rho T}]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}\zeta^{(2)}_{2,1}(\rho,t^{\prime},h^{\prime},h){\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\zeta^{(2)}_{2,1}(\rho,t^{\prime},h^{\prime},h){\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

(8.140)

\displaystyle:=\xi_{2,1}^{(2)}(\rho,v_{1},v_{2}),

which is a function of
\rho,v_{1},v_{2}
only.
∎

8.3.10.
Proof of Lemma

, type
\binom{2}{1,2}

Proof.

Using Lemma

, the mixed Palm expectations in (

) with

, we have

\displaystyle\frac{L^{(1)}_{1,2}}{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{1,2}^{(1)}}[e^{-\rho T}]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[e^{-\rho(T\circ\theta_{\hat{s}_{2}})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

(8.141)

\displaystyle\;+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[e^{-\rho(T\circ\theta_{\hat{s}_{2}})}\prod_{l=1,2}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h.

where
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})=(\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}))
is the second intersection of the birds at
(t_{n},h_{n})=(0,h),(t_{p},h_{p})=(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, which are of type
{\tau}_{n}=2
and
{\tau}_{p}=1
, respectively. We write this intersection
(\hat{s}_{2},\hat{h}_{2})
in short. Note that the station corresponding to the head point
(0,h)
is the serving station after time
\hat{s}_{2}
. Since
(0,h)
is of type
2
, we define
\hat{h}_{2}(t):=\left(v_{2}^{2}t^{2}+h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
, which we write in short as
\hat{h}_{t}
. Notice that

T\circ\theta_{\hat{s}_{2}}:=T_{1}(\hat{s}_{2})-\hat{s}_{2},

where
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{2})
is the handover that happens for the first time after time
\hat{s}_{2}
. The next handover point can be found by looking for the first head point, say
(T_{j},H_{j})
, the radial bird at which, intersects the one with head point
(0,h)
. Depending on the type of
(T_{j},H_{j})
there are two cases as follows:

Case 1.

(T_{j},H_{j})
is of type 1.
For the point
(T_{j},H_{j})
to be eligible for a handover, it must be outside
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
and satisfy
T_{j}\geq\hat{s}_{2}
. Otherwise, if
T_{j}<\hat{s}_{2}
, then the intersection between the birds with head at
(T_{j},H_{j})
and
(0,h)
, is either contained in
{\mathcal{L}}^{+}_{e}(v_{1},v_{2})
or it produces a handover before time
\hat{s}_{2}
. So it must be the case that
T_{j}\geq\hat{s}_{2}
and the unexplored region is
Q_{\hat{s}_{2}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
.

In addition to that, if
\hat{s}_{2}<0
, we must have
T_{j}\in D_{\ell}(0,h,H_{j})
or
T_{j}\in D_{r}(0,h,H_{j})
, when
\hat{s}_{2}\leq T_{j}\leq 0
and the type of the pair of consecutive handovers can be

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,2\\
1,2,1\end{array}\bigg]

or

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
1,2,1\end{array}\bigg]

, depending on whether
\hat{s}_{2}\leq T_{j}<0
or
T_{j}\geq 0
. If
\hat{s}_{2}\geq 0
, we must have
T_{j}\in D_{r}(0,h,H_{j})
and the type of the pair of consecutive handovers can be

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
1,2,1\end{array}\bigg]

only.
Suppose the two intersection points are
(s_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}))
and
(s_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),\hat{h}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}))
, both of which can be eligible for handovers. For the two intersection points to represent handovers we must have no point of
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
, for
l=1,2
, in the extra regions
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
and
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
, respectively, beyond
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
. Since
\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})\leq\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})
, we have

\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\subset\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}_{2}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}},

Hence, we choose the first intersection to be responsible for the next handover. Thus we have
T_{1}(\hat{s}_{2}):=\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})
and the extra region
{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}
has no points of
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
, for
l=1,2
, where
\hat{h}_{t}=(v_{2}^{2}t^{2}+h^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
, see Picture (
121
) of Figure
8.12
. This union is due to Definition
8.13
and to the fact that the individual sets in the union may not be monotonic.

Case 2.

(T_{j},H_{j})
is of type 2.
For the point
(T_{j},H_{j})
to be eligible, it must be from outside of
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
and satisfy
T_{j}\geq 0
. Otherwise if
T_{j}<0
, the two birds with heads at
(T_{j},H_{j})
and
(0,h)
intersect at a point which is contained in

, which is not interesting in this case. Thus we must have
T_{j}\geq 0
and and the unexplored region is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\

T_{1}(\hat{s}_{2}):=\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})
and the region

has no points of
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
, for
l=1,2
, where
\hat{h}_{t}=(v_{2}^{2}t^{2}+h^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{\exists(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\,\mbox{:}\,\hat{s}_{2}\leq T_{j}\leq 0,T_{j}\in D_{\ell}(0,h,H_{j}),\,\bigcap_{l=1,2}\left\{{\mathcal{H}}^{l}{\kern 0.0pt\left(\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)}^{\mathrm{o}}=0\right\}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{1}(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime},T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{2})}\right.{}

1,2,1\end{array}\bigg]$}+\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
