\displaystyle=2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\int_{R_{1}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{2})}e^{-2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\left|E^{\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})}\cup S_{1}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}e^{-2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\left|E^{\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{\rm d}t_{1}\,{\rm d}h_{1}{}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\int_{\frac{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}^{2}+v_{2}^{2}}\,\hat{s}_{2}}^{\hat{s}_{2}+\hat{h}_{2}/v_{1}}\int_{\left(\hat{h}_{2}^{2}-v_{1}^{2}(t_{1}-\hat{s}_{2})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\left(\frac{v_{1}^{2}v_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}t_{1}^{2}+h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{2})}e^{-2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\left|E^{\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})}\cup S_{1}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{}

\displaystyle\hskip 252.94499pt\times e^{-2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\left|E^{\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{\rm d}t_{1}\,{\rm d}h_{1}{}

\displaystyle\;\;+2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\int_{\hat{s}_{2}+\hat{h}_{2}/v_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\left(\frac{v_{1}^{2}v_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}t_{1}^{2}+h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{2})}e^{-2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\left|E^{\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})}\cup S_{1}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{}

(8.160)

\displaystyle\hskip 252.94499pt\times e^{-2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\left|E^{\hat{s}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{\rm d}t_{1}\,{\rm d}h_{1}.

For the third term in (
8.3.10
), using Remark
7.10
for
l=2
, the region satisfies

\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}=E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\cup S_{2}\cup E^{\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})},

where
\hat{h}_{t}=(v_{2}^{2}t^{2}+h^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
S_{2}
is the region outside
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\cup E^{\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})}
, but below the hyperbola given by the equation
u^{2}-\frac{v_{1}^{2}v_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}t^{2}=h^{2}
, in the
(t,u)
-coordinate system similar to (
7.17
) and within the time interval

\left[\frac{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}^{2}}\hat{s}_{2},\frac{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}^{2}}\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})\right].

For
l=2
, using the increasing property of
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\setminus E^{\hat{s},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}}\right\}_{t\geq\hat{s}_{2}}
from Part (
ii
) of Proposition
8.16
, we have that

\bigcup_{t\in[\hat{s}_{2},\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})]}E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{t}}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}}=E^{\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}}.

Then the third term in (
8.3.10
) equals,

\displaystyle\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
1,2,2\end{array}\bigg]$}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\sum_{(T_{j},H_{j})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,\mbox{:}\,T_{j}\geq 0}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\left(E^{\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})}\cup S_{2}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\left(E^{\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\right.{}

(8.163)

\displaystyle\hskip 289.07999pt\times e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,T_{j},H_{j})-\hat{s}_{2})}\Big].

The point
(T_{j},H_{j})
must lie in the region
R_{2}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\subset(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{2}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
, defined as

(8.164)

R_{2}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}):=\left\{(t,u)\,\mbox{:}\,t\geq 0,\,u\geq 0,\,v_{2}^{2}(t-\hat{s}_{2})^{2}+u^{2}\geq\hat{h}_{2}^{2}\right\}.

For the region
R_{2}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
in (
8.164
), the first condition
t\geq 0
is due to the discussion in
8.3.10
. The last two condition is due to the fact that the next head point must lie outside
E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}
. By the Campbell-Mecke formula for (
8.163
), we have

\displaystyle\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
1,2,2\end{array}\bigg]$}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\int_{R_{2}(h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{2})}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{1}\left(E^{\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})}\cup S_{2}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{l}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\right.{}

\displaystyle\hskip 216.81pt\left.\times\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\left(E^{\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}t_{1}{\rm d}h_{1}{}

\displaystyle=2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\int_{0}^{\hat{s}_{2}+\hat{h}_{2}/v_{2}}\int_{\left(\hat{h}_{2}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}(t_{1}-\hat{s}_{2})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\infty}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{2})}e^{-2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\left|E^{\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})}\cup S_{2}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{}

\displaystyle\hskip 224.03743pt\times e^{-2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\left|E^{\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{\rm d}t_{1}{\rm d}h_{1}{}

\displaystyle\;\;+2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\int_{\hat{s}_{2}+\hat{h}_{2}/v_{2}}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\rho(\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})-\hat{s}_{2})}e^{-2\lambda_{1}v_{1}\left|E^{\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{1}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})}\cup S_{2}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{}

(8.167)

\displaystyle\hskip 224.03743pt\times e^{-2\lambda_{2}v_{2}\left|E^{\hat{s}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1}),v_{2}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,t_{1},h_{1})}\cup E^{\hat{s}_{2},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{2}}\right|}{\rm d}t_{1}{\rm d}h_{1}.

Let us denote the sum of all terms in (
8.160
), (
8.167
) as
\zeta^{(1)}_{1,2}(\rho,t^{\prime},h^{\prime},h)=\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,2\\
1,2,1\end{array}\bigg]$}+\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
1,2,1\end{array}\bigg]$}+\zeta\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,1\\
1,2,2\end{array}\bigg]$}
. Using the expression for
\zeta^{(1)}_{1,2}(\rho,t^{\prime},h^{\prime},h)
in (
8.141
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{L^{(1)}_{1,2}}{4\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}v_{1}v_{2}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{1,2}^{(1)}}[e^{-\rho T}]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}\zeta^{(1)}_{1,2}(\rho,t^{\prime},h^{\prime},h){\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\zeta^{(1)}_{1,2}(\rho,t^{\prime},h^{\prime},h){\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

(8.168)

\displaystyle:=\xi_{1,2}^{(1)}(\rho,v_{1},v_{2}),

which is a function of
\rho,v_{1},v_{2}
only.
∎

In all these expressions, the common thread is the union of possibly overlapping region enclosed by half ellipses,
E^{s_{1},v}_{h_{1}}\cup E^{s_{2},v}_{h_{2}}
. We have computed the area of these regions in Appendix
A
, using the variables corresponding to the head points, and in particular for the regions of the form of
S_{1},S_{2}
.

Remark 8.23
.

Consider the multi-speed case with set of speeds
v_{[n]}
. Suppose
S^{v}_{1},S^{v}_{2}
are the regions defined as previously, for some speed
v\in v_{[n]}
. Then for all
v^{\prime}\in v_{[n]}
, such that
v^{\prime}>v
, the region
S^{v^{\prime}}_{1}\subset S^{v}_{1}
and
S^{v^{\prime}}_{2}\subset S^{v}_{2}
. For all
v^{\prime\prime}\in v_{[n]}
, such that
v^{\prime\prime}<v
, both
S^{v}_{1},S^{v}_{2}
are contained in the union of half ellipses
E^{\hat{s},v^{\prime\prime}}_{\hat{h}}\cup E^{\hat{s}^{\prime},v^{\prime\prime}}_{\hat{h}^{\prime}}
. This is the only point that needs to be taken separately in the multi-speed case, which is the content of Section
10
.

9.
Handover process as a factor of a Markov chain: two-speed case

Based on the information provided by Table
8.2
, one can construct a discrete Markov chain extending that of Section
6
to the two-speed case. Define the collection of types of handovers as

{\mathcal{T}}:=\left\{\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
i,j\end{array}\bigg]$}\,\mbox{:}\,q=1\text{ if }i=j\text{ and }q=1,2\text{ for }i\neq j,\text{ where }i,j\in\{1,2\}\right\}.

For each step
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, define
type(k)\in{\mathcal{T}}
to be the type of the handover at that step. The goal of this section is to construct a sequence
\left\{\left(H_{k}^{l},T^{r}_{k},H^{r}_{k}\right),type(k)\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
that forms a discrete time Markov chain on the state space
(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{3}\times{\mathcal{T}}
, where the sequence
\left\{\left(H_{k}^{l},T^{r}_{k},H^{r}_{k}\right)\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
of triples on
(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{3}
is determined as follows using the corresponding type and coordinate of the head points.
Heuristically, suppose the
k
-th handover is of type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
{\tau}_{p},{\tau}_{n}\end{array}\bigg]

and produced by the head points
(t^{p}_{k},h^{p}_{k})
and
(t^{n}_{k},h^{n}_{k})
, where the letter
p
and
n
stand for
previous
and
next
. Then we define

(9.1)

\displaystyle\left(H_{k}^{l},T^{r}_{k},H^{r}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}(h^{p}_{k},t^{n}_{k}-t^{p}_{k},h^{n}_{k})&\text{ if }q=1,\\
(h^{n}_{k},t^{p}_{k}-t^{n}_{k},h^{p}_{k}),&\text{ if }q=2.\end{cases}

The state at the
k
-th step implicitly depends on the previous step as the head point
(t^{p}_{k},h^{p}_{k})
is involved in previous handover(s). The following theorem establishes the Markovianity of the sequence
\left\{\left(H^{l}_{k},T^{r}_{k},H^{r}_{k}\right),type(k)\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
, following the foot steps of Theorem
6.1
.

Theorem 9.1
.

The sequence
\left\{\left(H^{l}_{k},T^{r}_{k},H^{r}_{k}\right),type(k)\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
forms a homogeneous Markov chain on the state space
(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{3}\times{\mathcal{T}}
.

Proof.

Fix
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. Let
type(k)=\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
i,j\end{array}\bigg]$}
be the type of the
k
-th handover, for some
i,j\in\{1,2\}
. Given
\left(H^{l}_{k},T^{r}_{k},H^{r}_{k}\right)=\left(h^{l}_{k},t^{r}_{k},h^{r}_{k}\right)
, suppose the locations of the responsible head points are
(0,h^{l}_{k})
and
(t^{r}_{k},h^{r}_{k})
, respectively and the intersection point of the corresponding radial birds for the handover in question is
(\hat{s}_{k},\hat{h}_{k})
. Define
{\tau}(t,h)
to be the type of a head point
(t,h)\in\mathbb{H}^{+}
. For any
s\in\mathbb{R}
, define
Q_{s}:=\{(t,h)\in\mathbb{H}^{+}:t\geq s\}
, to be the quadrant on
\mathbb{H}^{+}
and on the right of the vertical line
t=s
. Depending on the type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
i,j\end{array}\bigg]

, we can assign their corresponding type to the head points and carry out our analysis in the following two cases:

Case 1.

q=1
.
In this case, the head point corresponding to new station is on the right and so we have
{\tau}(t^{r}_{k},h^{r}_{k})=j
and
{\tau}(0,h^{l}_{k})=i
. Here, we have four possibilities for the handover types:
\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
i,j\end{array}\bigg]$}=\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
1,1\end{array}\bigg]$},\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
2,1\end{array}\bigg]$},\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
1,2\end{array}\bigg]$}
and

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
2,2\end{array}\bigg]

. Let
m\neq j\in\{1,2\}
.
The precise unexplored region for different cases plays an essential role in establishing the Markovianity of the process in question. To determine the
(k+1)
-st step of the process, one first needs to find the unexplored regions for potential location of the upcoming head point. Observe that there are two unexplored regions, as the upcoming head point can be of two different types. For any given type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
i,j\end{array}\bigg]

for the
k
-th handover, let
{\mathcal{U}}^{1}_{j}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{1}_{m}\subset\mathbb{H}^{+}
be the unexplored region of the upcoming head point of type
j
and
m
, respectively. Table
9.1
lists out the unexplored regions
{\mathcal{U}}^{1}_{j}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{1}_{m}
for the case
q=1
, by gathering the details in various parts of the proofs of Lemma
8.19
and Lemma
8.20
corresponding to different handover types. These unexplored regions only depend on the state
\left(\left(h^{l}_{k},t^{r}_{k},h^{r}_{k}\right),type(k)\right)
at step
k
.

Types:

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
i,j\end{array}\bigg]

{\mathcal{U}}^{1}_{j},{\mathcal{U}}^{1}_{m}
,
m\neq j

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
1,1\end{array}\bigg]

, Fig.

, Fig.

2,2\end{array}\bigg]

, Fig.

&

{\mathcal{U}}^{1}_{j}

Q_{t^{r}_{k}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}

Q_{t^{r}_{k}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}

Q_{t^{r}_{k}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}

Q_{t^{r}_{k}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}

{\mathcal{U}}^{1}_{m}

\mathbb{H}^{+}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}

Q_{0}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}

Q_{\hat{s}_{k}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}

Q_{\hat{s}_{k}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}

Table 9.1
.
Unexplored regions
{\mathcal{U}}^{1}_{j}
,

, for
m\neq j
, when the
k
-th handover type is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\

. The reference to figures are mentioned next to the handover types, resembles the corresponding scenario and they can be used to determine the unexplored regions.

As explained in (
8.36
) and Lemma
8.12

, one can construct a sequence of non-decreasing open sets
\{S^{j}_{t}\}_{t\geq\hat{s}_{k}}
and
\{S^{m}_{t}\}_{t\geq\hat{s}_{k}}

and
{\mathcal{U}}^{1}_{m}
, using the sequence

where

. By Corollary
8.14
, there exists a head point say
(T_{u},H_{u})
of type

in the unexplored region
{\mathcal{U}}^{1}_{j}
or
{\mathcal{U}}^{1}_{m}
such that
(k+1)
-st handover is given by the intersection point, say
\left(\hat{S}_{k+1},\hat{H}_{k+1}\right)
, of the radial birds at

and

. Here, the letter

stands for
upcoming
. The pair of stopping set is
\left(S^{j}_{\hat{S}_{k+1}},S^{m}_{\hat{S}_{k+1}}\right)
, and the upcoming head point
(T_{u},H_{u})
lie on the boundary
\partial S^{j}_{\hat{S}_{k+1}}
or
\partial S^{m}_{\hat{S}_{k+1}}
of the respective stopping sets, depending on whether

or

. Then the type of this handover is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q^{\prime}\\
j,{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]

, where

(9.2)

\displaystyle q^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }T_{u}\geq t^{r}_{k},\\
2,&\text{ if }T_{u}<t^{r}_{k}.\end{cases}

Subsequently we define

(9.3)

\displaystyle\left(H_{k+1}^{l},T^{r}_{k+1},H^{r}_{k+1}\right)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}(h^{r}_{k},T_{u}-t^{r}_{k},H_{u})&\text{ if }q^{\prime}=1,\\
(h^{r}_{k},t^{r}_{k}-T_{u},H_{u}),&\text{ if }q^{\prime}=2.\end{cases}

This is the ‘spatial’ part of the state at step
k+1
.

Case 2.

q=2
.
In this case, the head point corresponding to the new station is on the left and so we have
{\tau}(0,h^{l}_{k})=j
and
{\tau}(t^{r}_{k},h^{r}_{k})=i
. The possibilities of the handover types in this case are
\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
i,j\end{array}\bigg]$}=\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
2,1\end{array}\bigg]$}
and

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
1,2\end{array}\bigg]

. Let
m\neq j\in\{1,2\}
. The head point of the upcoming station can be found in the unexplored region, say
{\mathcal{U}}^{2}_{j}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{2}_{m}
, listed in the Table
9.2
in the case
q=2
, obtained using the same recipe as for Table
9.1
in the case
q=1
, as follows:

Types:

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
i,j\end{array}\bigg]

{\mathcal{U}}^{2}_{j},{\mathcal{U}}^{2}_{m}
,
m\neq j

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
2,1\end{array}\bigg]

, Fig.
8.10

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
1,2\end{array}\bigg]

, Fig.
8.9

{\mathcal{U}}^{2}_{j}

Q_{0}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}

Q_{0}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}

{\mathcal{U}}^{2}_{m}

Q_{t^{r}_{k}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}

Q_{\hat{s}_{k}}\setminus E^{\hat{s}_{k},v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{k}}

Table 9.2
.
Unexplored regions
{\mathcal{U}}^{2}_{j}
,
{\mathcal{U}}^{2}_{m}
, for
m\neq j
, when the

-th handover type is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
i,j\end{array}\bigg]

. The figures mentioned next to the handover types, are used to determine the unexplored regions.

These unexplored regions only depend on the state
\left(\left(h^{l}_{k},t^{r}_{k},h^{r}_{k}\right),type(k)\right)
at step
k
. Similarly to the case
q=1
, we construct a sequence of non-decreasing open sets
\{S^{j}_{t}\}_{t\geq\hat{s}_{k}}
and
\{S^{m}_{t}\}_{t\geq\hat{s}_{k}}
, representing explored regions in the unexplored region
{\mathcal{U}}^{2}_{j}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{2}_{m}
, using the sequence
\{h_{j}(t)\}_{t\geq\hat{s}_{k}}
, where
h_{j}(t):=\left(v_{j}^{2}t^{2}+(h^{l}_{k})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. By Corollary
8.14
, there exists a head point, say
(T_{u},H_{u})
, of type
{\tau}_{u}
in the unexplored region
{\mathcal{U}}^{2}_{j}
or
{\mathcal{U}}^{2}_{m}
such that
(k+1)
-st handover is given by the intersection point, say
\left(\hat{S}_{k+1},\hat{H}_{k+1}\right)
, of the radial birds at
(0,h^{l}_{k})
and
(T_{u},H_{u})
. The pair of stopping sets is
\left(S^{j}_{\hat{S}_{k+1}},S^{m}_{\hat{S}_{k+1}}\right)

(T_{u},H_{u})
lie on the boundary of the respective stopping sets, depending on whether
{\tau}_{u}=j
or
m
. From Table

, it is clear that

. Then, the type of the upcoming handover is

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q^{\prime}\\

, with

equal to
1
. Also note that
\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q^{\prime}\\
j,{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]$}=\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
j,j\end{array}\bigg]$}
is not possible for obvious reasons. Subsequently, we define the ‘spatial’ part of the state at
(k+1)
-st step as

(9.4)

\left(H_{k+1}^{l},T^{r}_{k+1},H^{r}_{k+1}\right):=(H^{r}_{k},T_{u}-t^{r}_{k},H_{u}).

The Markov property is satisfied by the sequence
\left\{\left(H^{l}_{k},T^{r}_{k},H^{r}_{k}\right),type(k)\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
, since the state at the
(k+1)
-st step,
\left(\left(H^{l}_{k+1},T^{r}_{k+1},H^{r}_{k+1}\right),type(k+1)\right)
, is determined using the unexplored regions,
{\mathcal{U}}^{1}_{j}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{1}_{m}
in
9
for
q=1
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{2}_{j}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{2}_{m}
in
9
for
q=2
, where one finds a Poisson point process of heads which is conditionally independent of the past, given the state
\left(\left(h^{l}_{k},t^{r}_{k},h^{r}_{k}\right),type(k)\right)
at step
k
. This completes the proof of Theorem
9.1
.
∎

Remark 9.2
.

Our technique allows one to predict the upcoming handover type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q^{\prime}\\
j,{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]

and the location of the responsible head point, say
(T_{u},H_{u})
of type
{\tau}_{u}
, given the state at step
k
. In addition, it enables us to derive a representation of
the transition kernel of the chain, for a transition to
\left((H^{l}_{k+1},T^{r}_{k+1},H^{r}_{k+1}),\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q^{\prime}\\
j,{\tau}_{u}\end{array}\bigg]$}\right)
, given the
k
-th state is
\left((H^{l}_{k},T^{r}_{k},H^{r}_{k}),\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
i,j\end{array}\bigg]$}\right)
along the line of the single-speed case.

10.
Handover Palm distribution and applications: multi-speed case

In this section we first determine the handover Palm probability distribution with the goal to determine the Palm probability distribution of inter-handover times and typical handover distance. An identical analysis follows to come up with a result similar to the mass transport principle (Lemma
5.2
for the single-speed and Lemma
8.1
for the two-speed case) for the multi-speed case.

10.1.
Palm probability with respect to handovers

Without loss of generality, here also we assume that
v_{1}>v_{2}>\cdots>v_{n}
. Identically to the two-speed case, here also the reference probability space is considered as
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}},\mathbb{P})
,
with
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}})
the canonical space of head point process
{\mathcal{H}}=\sum_{l\in[n]}{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
on
\mathbb{H}^{+}
, where
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}
is the head point process corresponding to speed
v_{l}
. A point
\omega\in\Omega
representing a realization of the point process
{\mathcal{H}}
. The measure space is equipped with the shift
\{\theta_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
along the time axis, defined as

(10.1)

\theta_{t}({\mathcal{H}})=\sum_{l\in[n]}\theta_{t}({\mathcal{H}}^{l})=\sum_{l\in[n]}\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\delta_{(T^{l}_{i}-t,H^{l}_{i})},

which is ergodic for
\mathbb{P}
. As in the two-speed case, the law
\mathbb{P}
of the head point process
{\mathcal{H}}
is left invariant by this shift.

We have constructed the handover point process
{\mathcal{V}}
in Subsubsection
7.2.3
, using the pure handover point processes
{\mathcal{V}}_{l}
for
l\in[n]
, and the mixed handover point process
{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{l,r}
for
l\neq r\in[n],k\in\{1,2\}
, depending on all possible type of intersections. The handover point process
{\mathcal{V}}
is stationary from Lemma
7.17
and has a positive and finite intensity from Theorem
7.20
. This allows us to define the Palm probability w.r.t.
{\mathcal{V}}
on the probability space
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}})
, which will be denoted as

\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}^{0}
.

Recall the point processes
{\mathcal{R}}_{l}
for
l\in[n]
and
{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{l,r}
for
l\neq r\in[n],k\in\{1,2\}
, corresponding to the right most head points. The same mass transport principle, Lemma
8.1
, under the bijections defined in Lemma
7.27
, applies to all the individual Palm probability measures, stated in the following.

Lemma 10.1
.

For all non-negative measurable functions
f
on
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}})
we have:

(i).

For all
l\in[n]
,

(10.2)

\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{l}}^{0}\left[f(\theta_{\beta_{l}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right],

where
\beta_{l}
is the bijection between the point processes
{\mathcal{V}}_{l}
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{l}
.

(ii).

For all
l\neq r\in[n],k=1,2
,

(10.3)

\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{l,r}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}_{l,r}}^{0}\left[f(\theta_{\beta^{(k)}_{l,r}(0)}({\mathcal{H}}))\right],

where
\beta^{(k)}_{l,r}
is the bijection between the point processes
{\mathcal{V}}_{l,r}^{(k)}
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{l,r}^{(k)}
, of type
\binom{k}{l,r}
.

Hence, we have the following decomposition of
\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]
, similar to Theorem
8.2
, based on different types of handovers, using the Palm probability measure for superposition of multi-type independent point processes (cf.
[
4
, Subsection 1.6, p. 36]
).

Theorem 10.2
.

For all non-negative measurable functions
f
on
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}})
,

(10.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]=\sum_{l\in[n]}\frac{\Lambda_{l}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]+\sum_{l\neq r\in[n],k=1,2}\frac{\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r}}{\lambda_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{l,r}}[f({\mathcal{H}})].

Similar to the supporting results, Lemma
8.3
, Lemma
8.4
, individual Palm probability measures are expressed as follows:

Lemma 10.3
.

For
l\in[n]
and for all non-negative measurable functions
f
on
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{l}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]

\displaystyle=\frac{4\lambda_{l}^{2}v_{l}^{2}}{\Lambda_{l}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{3}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\left[f\big(\theta_{\hat{s}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{l}+\delta_{(0,h)}+\delta_{(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}+\sum_{m\neq l}{\mathcal{H}}^{m})\big)\right.{}

\displaystyle\hskip 216.81pt\times\left.\prod_{i}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{i}\left(E^{\hat{s}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{i}}_{\hat{h}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\right]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h,{}

where
(\hat{s}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),\hat{h}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}))

are the coordinates of the intersection of the two radial birds with heads
(t,h)
and
(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
.

The proof Lemma
10.3
follows the steps of Lemma
5.3
and Lemma
8.3
, except for the extra condition, for the void probabilities, the multiple half-ellipses to be empty of head points corresponding to the different speeds, as in Theorem
7.29
and Figure
7.10
. Having said this, we move towards the interesting case of mixed handovers similar to Lemma
8.4
, for which we provide more details in the following.

Lemma 10.4
.

For
r\neq l\in[n],k=1,2
and for all non-negative measurable functions
f
on
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}})
,

\displaystyle\frac{\Lambda^{(k)}_{l,r}}{4\lambda_{l}\lambda_{r}v_{l}v_{r}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{l,r}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{t^{*}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\Big(\theta_{\hat{s}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{r}+\delta_{(0,h)}+{\mathcal{H}}^{l}+\delta_{(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}+\!\!\!\sum_{m\neq l,r}{\mathcal{H}}^{m})\Big){}

\displaystyle\hskip 180.67499pt\times\prod_{i}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{i}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{i}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h{}

\displaystyle\,+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg[f\Big(\theta_{\hat{s}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}({\mathcal{H}}^{r}+\delta_{(0,h)}+{\mathcal{H}}^{l}+\delta_{(-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}+\!\!\!\sum_{m\neq l,r}{\mathcal{H}}^{m})\Big){}

\displaystyle\hskip 180.67499pt\times\prod_{i}\mathbbmss{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{i}\left(E^{\hat{s}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),v_{i}}_{\hat{h}_{k}(0,h,-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\right)=0}\Bigg]{\rm d}t^{\prime}{\rm d}h^{\prime}{\rm d}h,{}

where,
(\hat{s}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),\hat{h}_{k}(t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}))

are the coordinates of the
k
-th intersection of the two radial birds with heads at
(t,h)
and
(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
,
t^{*}=\frac{1}{v_{l}v_{r}}(h^{2}-h^{\prime 2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{r}^{2}-v_{l}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

Remark 10.5
.

The symmetry envisioned earlier in Lemma
8.17
can also be shown to hold, using the same time-reversal argument, under the very specific assumption that the function
f
is symmetric, i.e.,
f({\mathcal{H}})=f(-{\mathcal{H}})
, defined in Subsubsection
8.1.1
. In other words,

\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(1)}_{i,j}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]=\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(2)}_{j,i}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]\text{ and }\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(2)}_{i,j}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]=\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(1)}_{j,i}}[f({\mathcal{H}})],

for all
i\neq j\in[n]
. Under the symmetric assumption on the function
f
, we have the decomposition of
\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]
from Theorem
10.2
that

(10.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{V}}}^{0}[f({\mathcal{H}})]=\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{\Lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{i}}[f({\mathcal{H}})]+2\sum_{j<i\in[n],k=1,2}\frac{\Lambda^{(k)}_{i,j}}{\lambda_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{(k)}_{i,j}}[f({\mathcal{H}})].

10.2.
Typical handover distance: multi-speed case

Let
\hat{H}
be the random variable for handover distance and
\hat{H}_{0}
be the distance of the typical handover. Based on the different types of intersections that happen at time
0
between the corresponding radial birds, we just state the following result for the Laplace transform of
\hat{H}^{2}
under the Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{0}_{\mathcal{V}}
, in the multi-speed setting. This can be proved along the same lines of Lemma
8.9
, by applying the same ideas along with the formula (
10.5
) and the symmetry, adapted to the multi-speed setting.

Lemma 10.6
.

For any
\gamma\geq 0
, the Laplace transform of
\hat{H}^{2}
under the Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{V}}^{0}
of handover is

(10.6)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}^{0}_{\hat{H}^{2}}(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\frac{4\sqrt{\lambda}}{\pi}\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{\lambda\pi}\right)^{-3/2}\sum_{i\in[n]}v_{i}\left(\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}+\frac{8}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\sum_{j<i\in[n],k\in\{1,2\}}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}v_{i}v_{j}\,\eta^{(k)}_{i,j}(\gamma),

where
\eta^{(k)}_{i,j}(\gamma)
can be determined as in (
8.30
) and (
8.31
) for
j<i\in[n]
and
k\in\{1,2\}
and
\Lambda_{i,j}^{(k)}=4\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}v_{i}v_{j}\,\eta^{(k)}_{i,j}(0)
, for
k=1,2
.

10.3.
Distribution of inter-handover time: multi-speed case

Let us first convert the handover types bijectively to the new set of notations

{\mathcal{T}}_{n}:=\left\{\scalebox{0.7}{$\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
l,m\end{array}\bigg]$}:q=1\text{ if }l=m,\text{ and }q=1,2\text{ for }l\neq m,l,m\in[n]\right\},

using a table similar to Table
8.1
for the
n
types of speeds. As a result for any
l\in[n]
, there exists a handover type
l
or equivalently

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
l,l\end{array}\bigg]

, and pure handover point process
{\mathcal{V}}_{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\mathcal{W}}_{l}
. Also, for any
i\neq j\in[n]
and
k\in\{1,2\}
, there exists
l\neq m\in[n]
and
q\in\{1,2\}
such that,
{\mathcal{V}}_{i,j}^{(k)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\mathcal{W}}^{(q)}_{l,m}
, where the left hand side correspond to handover type
\binom{k}{i,j}
(in the old notations) and right hand side correspond to handover type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
l,m\end{array}\bigg]

. With this in hand, we write the handover point process as

{\mathcal{V}}\equiv{\mathcal{W}}:=\sum_{i\in[n]}{\mathcal{W}}_{i}+\sum_{l\neq m\in[n],q\in\{1,2\}}{\mathcal{W}}^{(q)}_{l,m}.

Let
L_{l}
be the intensity of the pure handover process
{\mathcal{W}}_{l}
, for
l\in[n]
and
L^{(q)}_{l,m}
be the intensity of the mixed handover process
{\mathcal{W}}^{(q)}_{l,m}
of type

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}q\\
l,m\end{array}\bigg]

, for
l\neq m\in[n]
,
q\in\{1,2\}
. Then we have the decomposition of the Laplace transform of the inter-handover time
T
, identical to Theorem
8.38
, under the Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{0}_{\mathcal{W}}\equiv\mathbb{P}^{0}_{\mathcal{V}}
.

Theorem 10.7
.

Let
\rho\geq 0
. In the multi-speed case, the Laplace transform of
T
under the Palm probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{0}_{{\mathcal{V}}}
is given by:

(10.7)

\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{W}}}^{0}[e^{-\rho T}]=\sum_{l\in[n]}\frac{L_{l}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{W}}}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{l}}^{0}[e^{-\rho T}]+\sum_{l\neq m\in[n],k\in\{1,2\}}\frac{L_{l,m}^{(q)}}{\lambda_{{\mathcal{W}}}}\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}^{(q)}_{l,m}}[e^{-\rho T}].

Proof of Theorem
10.7
.

The result can be proved similarly to Theorem
8.38
.
We derive the expressions for
\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}_{l}}[e^{-\rho T}]
, for
l\in[n]
and
\mathbb{E}^{0}_{{\mathcal{W}}^{(q)}_{l,m}}[e^{-\rho T}]
, for
l\neq m\in[n],q\in\{1,2\}
, with the help of Lemma
10.3
and Lemma
10.4
, following the same steps as those of Lemma
8.19
and Lemma
8.20
in the two-speed case, adapted to the multi-speed setting, along with the help of Remark
8.23
.
∎

11.
Future work

Related problems.
Our model and the corresponding results are the first steps towards understanding the handover phenomenon in a model with mobile stations and static user, through the dynamical Poisson-Voronoi tessellation in the simplest case. One can gradually relax the simplifications along the following line of research on far richer class of dynamical tessellations.

1.

Random environment
: Exploring the motion of the stations on a random environment, for example on the Poisson line process, in the single and the multi-speed settings.

2.

Higher dimensions
: Generalizing all these models to
3
and higher dimensions in both single and multiple speed scenarios.

3.

Finite visibility
: Understanding all these models in
2
and higher dimension, where the user or the stations or both have finite visibility. This restriction to the user and stations is not so unrealistic, as it naturally appears in the spherical case.

4.

Spherical case
: It would be interesting to analyze handovers in the spherical case, where stations are moving along randomly oriented orbits, as in the communication model with LEO and MEO constellations. We believe that the tools of spherical stochastic geometry and the knowledge acquired in all the scenarios defined in the present paper, will certainly help us understand this spherical case in the future.

5.

Markov structure and more
: In general, the Markov structure described in Section
6
and Section
9
for the handover process should allow one to derive many structural properties of the process, e.g. CLT, LLN and large deviations, for all the models defined above.

6.

Other distance metrics
: It would be interesting to determine the handover frequency for a general class of dynamic tessellations, where, instead of the Euclidean metric, one uses other metrics, for example:
\ell_{p}
-distances, Manhattan distance, supremum distance. This can be considered as a more abstract mathematical problem.

7.

Other dynamical tessellations
: The dynamical versions of the Voronoi tessellations defined in
[
22
]
would be important extensions of this article.

8.

Other handover protocols
: One can define the handover in terms of SINR based association rule and answer same questions in this case.

9.

Multi-hop association rule
: In the architecture of communication networks involving satellites, a user is served by an anchor station via multiple satellites connected through inter-satellite-links. Change of one of the connections: user to satellite, satellite to satellite or satellite to anchor station, leads to handovers of different types. Analyzing the geometric and statistical properties of this handover process in this practical setup and for all the models described above, is an important goal of the line of research.

10.

Other types of dynamics
: In this article we have looked at the dynamics of the stations according to a simple way-point model, where the motion is without change of direction and speed. One can consider other classes of dynamics for the stations according to, for example, more general random way-point models, jump processes, Brownian motion, diffusion processes etc., as long as the displacement theorem holds true for the underlying point process of stations. This gives rise to a rich classes of dynamical tessellations where a similar analysis would be of interest.

Appendix A

A.1.
Displacement theorem for the Poisson point process

For some
t>0
, define the independent random
displacement map

D_{t}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(-\pi,\pi]\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and direction
\theta\in(-\pi,\pi]
,

D_{t}(x,\theta):=x+v_{x}t,

where
v_{x}:=(v\cos\theta,v\sin\theta)
, is the velocity vector of the atom at
x
. The displacements are all i.i.d. but all equal in norm for every atom and fixed
t
. For any
t>0
, the
displacement kernel

\kappa_{t}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(-\pi,\pi]\times{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
, is defined as

(1)

\kappa_{t}(x,\theta,B):=\mathbb{P}(D_{t}(x,\theta)\in B|\tilde{\Phi})=\mathbb{P}(x+v_{x}t\in B|\tilde{\Phi})=\mathbbmss{1}_{\{x+v_{x}t\in B\}},

for any
B\in{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, initial location
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and direction of motion
\theta\in(-\pi,\pi]
, where
{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
is the Borel
\sigma
-algebra on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Since
\kappa_{t}(x,\theta,\mathbb{R}^{2})=1
, the measure
\kappa_{t}(x,\theta,\cdot)
is a probability kernel for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\theta\in(-\pi,\pi]
, see the
[
3
, definition 14.D.1]
.

Proposition A.1
.

For any
t>0
, the following statements are true:

(i)

The displacement kernel
\kappa_{t}
preserves the Lebesgue measure.

(ii)

The point process
\tilde{\Phi}^{t}
has the same distribution as
\Phi
.

Proof of Proposition
A.1
(
i
).

For the first part, let
B
be a Borel subset of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\theta\in(-\pi,\pi]
. The displacement kernel is
\kappa_{t}(x,\theta,B)=\mathbbmss{1}_{\{x+v_{x}t\in B\}}
. In turn, the intensity measure of the point process
\tilde{\Phi}^{t}
becomes

(2)

\displaystyle m_{\tilde{\Phi}^{t}}(B)=\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Phi}^{t}(B)]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbbmss{1}_{\{Y_{i}\in B\}}|\tilde{\Phi}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbmss{1}_{\{Y_{i}\in B\}}|\tilde{\Phi}\right]\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\kappa_{t}(X_{i},\Theta_{i},B)\right].

Applying the Campbell-Mecke formula in (
2
), we get

(3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\kappa_{t}(X_{i},\Theta_{i},B)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(-\pi,\pi]}\kappa_{t}(x,\theta,B)m_{\tilde{\Phi}}({\rm d}x,{\rm d}\theta)\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\kappa_{t}(x,\theta,B)\frac{\lambda}{2\pi}{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\theta.

Using the displacement kernel from (
1
) the last term in (
3
) equals to

(4)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\mathbbmss{1}_{\{x+v_{x}t\in B\}}{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\theta

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{2\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbbmss{1}_{\{x\in B-v_{x}t\}}{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}\theta=\frac{\lambda}{2\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}|B-v_{x}t|{\rm d}\theta=\lambda|B|.

Thus the intensity measure
m_{\tilde{\Phi}^{t}}
of the point process
\tilde{\Phi}^{t}
also has Lebesgue density.
∎

Proof of Proposition
A.1
(
ii
).

For the second part, since the displacements are i.i.d., by the displacement theorem
[
3
, Theorem 2.2.17]
, the point process
\tilde{\Phi}^{t}
is Poisson. This can be seen through the Laplace transform of the point process
\tilde{\Phi}^{t}
. Let us enumerate the points of
\Phi
and
\Phi^{t}
respectively as
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{Y_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
respectively. Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a measurable function. Then the Laplace transform of the function
f
with respect to
\tilde{\Phi}^{t}
is

(5)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!{\mathcal{L}}_{\tilde{\Phi}^{t}}(f):=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp{\left(-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f\,{\rm d}\tilde{\Phi}^{t}\right)}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}f(Y_{i})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i\in\mathbb{N}}e^{-f(Y_{i})}\right].

Conditioning with respect to the point process
\tilde{\Phi}
for last term in (
5
), we have

(6)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i\in\mathbb{N}}e^{-f(Y_{i})}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i\in\mathbb{N}}e^{-f(Y_{i})}|\tilde{\Phi}\right]\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-f(y)}\kappa_{t}(X_{i},\Theta_{i},{\rm d}y)\right].

From (
1
) we write,
\kappa_{t}(X_{i},\Theta_{i},{\rm d}y)=1_{\{X_{i}+V_{i}t\in{\rm d}y\}}
. Let us now define a measurable function
g:\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(-\pi,\pi]\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
using the inner integral in (
6
),

(7)

\displaystyle g(x,\theta)

\displaystyle=-\log\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-f(y)}\kappa_{t}(x,\theta,{\rm d}y).

Using the function
g
from (
7
) in (
6
) we obtain the Laplace transform as

(8)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\tilde{\Phi}^{t}}(f)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp{\left(-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}g(X_{i},\Theta_{i})\right)}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp{\left(-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(-\pi,\pi]}g{\rm d}\tilde{\Phi}\right)}\right]={\mathcal{L}}_{\tilde{\Phi}}(g).

Evaluating the Laplace transform
{\mathcal{L}}_{\tilde{\Phi}}(g)
of the function
g
with respect to the marked Poisson point process
\tilde{\Phi}
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\tilde{\Phi}}(g)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\left(1-e^{-g(x,\theta)}\right)m_{\tilde{\Phi}}({\rm d}x,{\rm d}\theta)\right){}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq:laplace-tphi})}}{{=}}\exp\left(-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\left(1-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-f(y)}\mathbbmss{1}_{\{x+v_{x}t\in{\rm d}y\}}\right)m_{\tilde{\Phi}}({\rm d}x,{\rm d}\theta)\right){}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(Prop.~\ref{proposition:Phi-Phi-t}(\ref{Phi-Phi-t1}))}}{{=}}\exp\left(-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(1-e^{-f(y)}\right)\mathbbmss{1}_{\{x+v_{x}t\in{\rm d}y\}}m_{\tilde{\Phi}}({\rm d}x,{\rm d}\theta)\right){}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(Fubini)}}{{=}}\exp\left(-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(1-e^{-f(y)}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\mathbbmss{1}_{\{x+v_{x}t\in{\rm d}y\}}m_{\tilde{\Phi}}({\rm d}x,{\rm d}\theta)\right){}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq:preserve1})}}{{=}}\exp\left(-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(1-e^{-f(y)}\right)m_{\tilde{\Phi}^{t}}({\rm d}y)\right){}

(9)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(Prop.~\ref{proposition:Phi-Phi-t}\,(\ref{Phi-Phi-t1}))}}{{=}}\exp\left(-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(1-e^{-f(y)}\right)\lambda{\rm d}y\right)={\mathcal{L}}_{\Phi}(f),

since from part (
i
), we get that
m_{\tilde{\Phi}^{t}}
has Lebesgue density. We obtain from (
8
) and (
9
) that
{\mathcal{L}}_{\tilde{\Phi}^{t}}(f)={\mathcal{L}}_{\Phi}(f)
and hence by the characterization of the Poisson point process, the point process
\tilde{\Phi}^{t}
corresponding to the new locations is also Poisson.
∎

A.3.
Proof of Proposition
8.16

We first provide the justification for the claim for the three type of handovers at time

, for
{\tau}_{n}=1
, i.e., when the station that takes over at time

is of type

. This corresponds to intersections of types

,

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\

and
\binom{1}{1,2}
or

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
2,1\end{array}\bigg]

, respectively. Let us start the proof of Part (
i
) for these cases.

Proof of Proposition
8.16
, Part (
i
), typical handover type
\binom{1}{2,1}
or equivalently

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
2,1\end{array}\bigg]

.

Suppose the handover happens at a distance
\hat{h}_{0}
, which is given by the first intersection of two radial birds with head points
(t_{n},h_{n})=(t_{1},h_{1})
of type
{\tau}_{n}=1
and
(t_{p},h_{p})=(t_{2},h_{2})
of type
{\tau}_{p}=2
, such that
t_{1}\geq t_{2}
. By construction,
(t_{1},h_{1})
lies on
\partial E^{0,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
and
(t_{2},h_{2})
lies on
\partial E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
. In fact, for any
(t_{1},h_{1})
lying on
\partial E^{0,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
and
(t_{2},h_{2})
lying on
\partial E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
, we get the same handover. Given
\hat{h}_{0},t_{1},h_{1}
, define
\hat{h}_{1}(t):=(v_{1}^{2}(t-t_{1})^{2}+h_{1}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
and consider the two collections
\left\{E^{t,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
and
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
, of upper half ellipses
E^{t,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}
and
E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}
with their top point lying on the radial bird
C^{1}_{(t_{1},h_{1})}
.

Case 1.

\left\{E^{t,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
.
It can be proved, using the techniques applied for the single-speed case, that the collection
\left\{E^{t,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
is non-decreasing in
t
, since the boundaries
\partial E^{t,v_{1}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}
pass through
(t_{1},h_{1})
, for all
t
, see Picture
31
of Figure
A.3
.

t
\bullet
(t_{2},h_{2})

Monotonicity property of the collection

.

Case 2.

\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
. We prove this case in two parts. In the first part, for

, using the

, as described in Remark
8.17
,
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq t_{1}}

We consider now the part where

, which corresponds to the decreasing wing of the radial bird

. We first claim that, given
(t_{1},h_{1})
, there exists
\tilde{t}>0
such that, for all
t<\tilde{t}
,

and for all

,

. Indeed, the critical value

is given by the non-trivial solution of the equation

, which is either

or
\frac{2}{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}(v_{1}^{2}t_{1}-v_{2}\hat{h}_{0})
. Define,

(10)

\tilde{t}=\begin{cases}0,&\text{ if }v_{1}^{2}t_{1}\leq v_{2}\hat{h}_{0},\\
\frac{2}{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}(v_{1}^{2}t_{1}-v_{2}\hat{h}_{0}),&\text{ if }v_{1}^{2}t_{1}>v_{2}\hat{h}_{0}.\end{cases}

Given
v_{1},v_{2},\hat{h}_{0}
, define

(11)

t^{*}:=\begin{cases}0,&\text{ if }v_{1}^{2}t_{1}\leq v_{2}\hat{h}_{0},\\
\frac{2v_{2}\hat{h}_{0}}{v_{1}^{2}+v_{2}^{2}},&\text{ if }v_{1}^{2}t_{1}>v_{2}\hat{h}_{0}.\end{cases}

As a side remark, observe that
t^{*}
is given by an intersection of the line segment joining the two points
(0,\hat{h}_{0})
and
(\hat{h}_{0}/v_{2},0)
and
\partial E_{\hat{h}_{0}}^{0,v_{1}}
, which are
(0,\hat{h}_{0})
and

.

Subcase 2.1.

v_{1}^{2}t_{1}>v_{2}\hat{h}_{0}
. In this case
\tilde{t}=\frac{2}{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}(v_{1}^{2}t_{1}-v_{2}\hat{h}_{0})
and
t^{*}=\frac{2v_{2}\hat{h}_{0}}{v_{1}^{2}+v_{2}^{2}}
. One can show from (
10
) that, for all
(t_{1},h_{1})\in\partial E_{\hat{h}_{0}}^{0,v_{1}}
such that, for all
t_{1}\in[0,t^{*}]
, we have
\tilde{t}\leq t_{1}
and for all
t_{1}\in(t^{*},\hat{h}_{0}/v_{1}]
, we have
\tilde{t}>t_{1}
. Indeed, if
t_{1}\in(t^{*},\hat{h}_{0}/v_{1}]
, then there does not exist any radial bird of type
2
with head point lying outside
E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
that intersects the radial bird
C^{1}_{(t_{1},h_{1})}
, twice on its decreasing wing. We perform further analysis by dividing in two cases:
(t_{1},h_{1})
being such that
t_{1}\in(t^{*},\hat{h}_{0}/v_{1}]
or
t_{1}\in[0,t^{*}]
.

Subsubcase 2.1.1.

t_{1}\in(t^{*},\hat{h}_{0}/v_{1}]
. In this case, by definition of
t^{*}
, we have
\tilde{t}>t_{1}
and by definition of
\tilde{t}
,
E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}(t)}\subset E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
, for all
t<\tilde{t}
and in particular for all
t<t_{1}
.
Also, since
\tilde{t}>t_{1}
, for
t_{1}\in(t^{*},\hat{h}_{0}/v_{1}]
,
\tilde{t}
corresponds to the increasing wing of the bird
C^{1}_{(t_{1},h_{1})}
. Hence, using the
increasing wing property
, as described in Remark
8.17
, the collection of open sets
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq t_{1}}
is non-decreasing, and so is
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq\tilde{t}}
in particular. Also by definition of
\tilde{t}
,
E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}=\emptyset
, for all
t\in[0,\tilde{t}]
. As a whole, the collection
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
is non-decreasing.

Subsubcase 2.1.2.

t_{1}\in[0,t^{*}]
. Here again, by definition of
t^{*}
, we have
\tilde{t}<t_{1}
. We are interested in understanding the behavior of the collection
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\in[\tilde{t},t_{1}]}
. It only concerns the interval
[\tilde{t},t_{1}]
, because
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq t_{1}}
is anyway non-decreasing using the
increasing wing property
, as in Remark
8.17
and
E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}=\emptyset
, for
t\in[0,\tilde{t}]
. Let us present a general argument for the monotonicity property of interest for the interval
[\tilde{t},t_{1}]
.

Let us take a time point
{\tau}\in[\tilde{t},t_{1})
and choose
\varepsilon>0
arbitrarily small. The boundaries of
\partial E^{\tau,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(\tau)}
and
\partial E^{\tau+\varepsilon,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(\tau+\varepsilon)}
are given by the equations

(12)

v_{2}^{2}(t-\tau)^{2}+h^{2}=\hat{h}_{1}(\tau)^{2},\,\,v_{2}^{2}(t-\tau-\varepsilon)^{2}+h^{2}=\hat{h}_{1}(\tau+\varepsilon)^{2},

respectively, where

(13)

\hat{h}_{1}({\tau})=\left(v_{1}^{2}({\tau}-t_{1})^{2}+h_{1}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\,\,\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)=\left(v_{1}^{2}({\tau}+\varepsilon-t_{1})^{2}+h_{1}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Suppose
(\bar{t},\bar{h})\equiv(\bar{t}({\tau},\varepsilon),\bar{h}({\tau},\varepsilon))\in\mathbb{H}^{+}
is an intersection point, if it exists, of the two ellipses in (
12
). We have the following proposition:

Proposition A.2
.

For any
{\tau}
and
\varepsilon>0
, with
\tilde{t}\leq{\tau}<t_{1}
, the following holds true:

(a).

(\bar{t},\bar{h})
exists and
(\bar{t},\bar{h})\in E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
,

(b).

E^{{\tau},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}({\tau})}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\subset E^{{\tau}+\varepsilon,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
.

t
\bullet
(t_{2},h_{2})
\bullet
(t_{1},h_{1})
(0,\hat{h}_{0})
E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
{\tau}
E^{{\tau},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}({\tau})}
{\tau}+\varepsilon
E^{{\tau}+\varepsilon,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)}
(\bar{t},\bar{h})

Figure A.4
.
For any
{\tau}\in[\tilde{t},t_{1})
and the intersection point
(\bar{t},\bar{h})
of the two boundaries
\partial E^{{\tau},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}({\tau})}
and
\partial E^{{\tau}+\varepsilon,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)}
, belongs to
E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
.

By Proposition
A.2
, the collection of sets
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\in[\tilde{t},t_{1}]}
is non-decreasing, in particular. Finally, the sequence
\left\{E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\right\}_{t\geq 0}
is non-decreasing.

Subcase 2.2.

v_{1}^{2}t_{1}\leq v_{2}\hat{h}_{0}
. Then
t^{*}=0
, and
\tilde{t}=0
as well. By definition of
\tilde{t}
,
E^{{\tau},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}({\tau})}\cap(E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}})^{c}\neq\emptyset
, for all
{\tau}\geq 0
. We establish the claim using the same steps of
A.3
, along with the help of Proposition
A.2
, with
\tilde{t}=0
. We do not provide the complete proof of Proposition
A.2
, with
\tilde{t}=0
. As a matter of fact, we just show that
(\hat{h}_{1}(0))^{{}^{\prime}}\geq-v_{2}
. The last inequality holds true, since from (
17
), we have

(14)

\displaystyle(\hat{h}_{1}(0))^{{}^{\prime}}=-\frac{v_{1}^{2}t_{1}}{\hat{h}_{0}}\geq-v_{2},

using the assumption
v_{1}^{2}t_{1}\leq v_{2}\hat{h}_{0}
of this
A.3
.

This completes the proof of Part (
i
) of Proposition
8.16
, when the typical handover is of type
\binom{1}{2,1}
or equivalently

\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
2,1\end{array}\bigg]

.
∎

Proof of Proposition
A.2
, Part (
a
).

We first prove that the two boundaries
\partial E^{\tau,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(\tau)}
and
\partial E^{\tau+\varepsilon,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(\tau+\varepsilon)}
intersect for any
0\leq{\tau}<t_{1}
and arbitrarily small
\varepsilon>0
. For this, it is enough to show that,
\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)\leq\hat{h}_{1}({\tau})
and

(15)

\displaystyle{\tau}+\varepsilon+\frac{1}{v_{2}}\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)\geq{\tau}+\frac{1}{v_{2}}\hat{h}_{1}({\tau})\text{ i.e., }\frac{\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)-\hat{h}_{1}({\tau})}{\varepsilon}\geq-v_{2},

i.e., the right most point
({\tau}+\varepsilon+\frac{1}{v_{2}}\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon),0)
of the upper half-ellipse
E^{\tau+\varepsilon,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(\tau+\varepsilon)}
, is on the right of the right-most point
({\tau}+\frac{1}{v_{2}}\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}),0)
of the upper half-ellipse
E^{\tau,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(\tau)}
.
The first inequality
\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)\leq\hat{h}_{1}({\tau})
is true since,
{\tau}<t_{1}
, which correspond to the decreasing wing of
C^{1}_{(t_{1},h_{1})}
.
The second inequality (
15
) is true if we can show that
(\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}))^{\prime}\geq-v_{2}
for all
0\leq{\tau}<t_{1}
, where

(16)

\displaystyle\left(\hat{h}_{1}({\tau})\right)^{{}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{1}^{2}({\tau}-t_{1})}{\hat{h}_{1}({\tau})}.

If we can show that
(\hat{h}_{1}(\tilde{t}))^{\prime}\geq-v_{2}
, then, by the property of the decreasing wing of the radial bird
C^{1}_{(t_{1},h_{1})}
, we have
(\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}))^{\prime}>(\hat{h}_{1}(\tilde{t}))^{\prime}\geq-v_{2}
, for all
{\tau}\in[\tilde{t},t_{1})
. Indeed, using the equality
\tilde{t}+\hat{h}_{1}(\tilde{t})/v_{2}=\hat{h}_{0}/v_{2}
, we get

(17)

\displaystyle\left(\hat{h}_{1}(\tilde{t})\right)^{\prime}=\frac{v_{1}^{2}(\tilde{t}-t_{1})}{\hat{h}_{1}(\tilde{t})}=\frac{v_{1}^{2}(\tilde{t}-t_{1})}{\hat{h}_{0}-v_{2}\tilde{t}}.

We have
\frac{v_{1}^{2}(\tilde{t}-t_{1})}{\hat{h}_{0}-v_{2}\tilde{t}}\geq-v_{2}
, since

(18)

\displaystyle v_{1}^{2}(\tilde{t}-t_{1})+v_{2}(\hat{h}_{0}-v_{2}\tilde{t})=(v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2})\left(\tilde{t}-\frac{1}{v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}(v_{1}^{2}t_{1}-v_{2}\hat{h}_{0})\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq:tilde-t})}}{{=}}v_{1}^{2}t_{1}-v_{2}\hat{h}_{0}\geq 0.

This proves the inequality (
15
) and as a result shows the existence of the intersection between the two boundaries
\partial E^{\tau,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(\tau)}
and
\partial E^{\tau+\varepsilon,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(\tau+\varepsilon)}
.

We find the coordinate of the intersection
(\bar{t},\bar{h})
of the two ellipses using the governing equations in (
12
), where

(19)

\bar{t}={\frac{1}{2}}\left[2{\tau}+\varepsilon+\frac{\hat{h}_{1}^{2}({\tau})-\hat{h}^{2}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)}{v_{2}^{2}\varepsilon}\right],\,\,\bar{h}^{2}=\hat{h}_{1}^{2}({\tau})-v_{2}^{2}(\bar{t}-{\tau})^{2}=\hat{h}_{1}^{2}({\tau})-\frac{v_{2}^{2}}{4}\left[\varepsilon+\frac{\hat{h}_{1}^{2}({\tau})-\hat{h}^{2}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)}{v_{2}^{2}\varepsilon}\right]^{2}.

Let us first prove that for any
{\tau}
, with
\tilde{t}\leq{\tau}<t_{1}
,
(\bar{t},\bar{h})\in E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
, i.e.,
0<v_{2}^{2}\bar{t}^{2}+\bar{h}^{2}<\hat{h}_{0}^{2}
. Using the expressions of
\bar{t},\bar{h}^{2}
from (
19
) we get that,

\displaystyle v_{2}^{2}\bar{t}^{2}+\bar{h}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{2}^{2}}{4}\left[2{\tau}+\varepsilon+\frac{\hat{h}_{1}^{2}({\tau})-\hat{h}^{2}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)}{v_{2}^{2}\varepsilon}\right]^{2}+\hat{h}_{1}^{2}({\tau})-\frac{v_{2}^{2}}{4}\left[\varepsilon+\frac{\hat{h}_{1}^{2}({\tau})-\hat{h}^{2}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)}{v_{2}^{2}\varepsilon}\right]^{2}{}

(20)

\displaystyle=v_{2}^{2}{\tau}\left[{\tau}+\varepsilon+\frac{\hat{h}_{1}^{2}({\tau})-\hat{h}^{2}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)}{v_{2}^{2}\varepsilon}\right]+\hat{h}_{1}^{2}({\tau})

(21)

\displaystyle=v_{2}^{2}{\tau}({\tau}+\varepsilon)+\frac{{\tau}+\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}\hat{h}_{1}^{2}({\tau})-\frac{{\tau}}{\varepsilon}\hat{h}_{1}^{2}({\tau}+\varepsilon).

Now, due to the fact that
({\tau},\hat{h}_{1}({\tau})),({\tau}+\varepsilon,\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon))\in C^{1}_{(t_{1},h_{1})}
, using the expressions for
\hat{h}_{1}({\tau})
and
\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)

from (
13
) to the last expression in (
21
), we obtain that

\displaystyle v_{2}^{2}\bar{t}^{2}+\bar{h}^{2}

\displaystyle=v_{2}^{2}{\tau}({\tau}+\varepsilon)+\frac{{\tau}+\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}\left[v_{1}^{2}({\tau}-t_{1})^{2}+h_{1}^{2}\right]-\frac{{\tau}}{\varepsilon}\left[v_{1}^{2}({\tau}+\varepsilon-t_{1})^{2}+h_{1}^{2}\right]{}

\displaystyle=v_{2}^{2}{\tau}({\tau}+\varepsilon)+h_{1}^{2}+v_{1}^{2}(t_{1}^{2}-{\tau}({\tau}+\varepsilon)){}

(22)

\displaystyle=h_{1}^{2}+v_{1}^{2}t_{1}^{2}-(v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}){\tau}({\tau}+\varepsilon)=\hat{h}_{0}^{2}-(v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}){\tau}({\tau}+\varepsilon),

since
h_{1}^{2}+v_{1}^{2}t_{1}^{2}=\hat{h}_{0}^{2}
. The quantity in the last step of (
22
) is less than
\hat{h}_{0}^{2}
, since
v_{1}>v_{2}
and greater than
0
, which is clear from (
20
), since
{\tau}<t_{1}
and
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrarily small. This implies
(\bar{t},\bar{h})\in E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
, see Figure
A.4
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
A.2
, Part (
b
).

Let us take
{\tau}\in[\tilde{t},t_{1})
and
\varepsilon>0
arbitrarily small. Then
(\bar{t},\bar{h})
being the intersection of
\partial E^{\tau,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(\tau_{1})}
and
\partial E^{\tau+\varepsilon,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(\tau+\varepsilon)}
, implies that for all
t^{\prime}<\bar{t}
, we have
v_{2}^{2}(t^{\prime}-\tau)^{2}+h^{2}<v_{2}^{2}(t^{\prime}-\tau-\varepsilon)^{2}+h^{2}
and for all
t^{\prime}>\bar{t}
, we have
v_{2}^{2}(t^{\prime}-\tau)^{2}+h^{2}>v_{2}^{2}(t^{\prime}-\tau-\varepsilon)^{2}+h^{2}
, using the curves as in (
12
) of the boundaries, see Figure
A.4
. This along with the fact that
(\bar{t},\bar{h})\in E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
, allows one to conclude that
E^{{\tau},v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}({\tau})}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\subset E^{{\tau}+\varepsilon,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}({\tau}+\varepsilon)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
. This justifies the claim.
∎

Remark A.3
.

The converse of the argument used in the proof of Proposition
A.2
, part (
b
), can be proved to be false in general case, using a counter example depicted in Picture
32
of Figure
A.3
. Indeed, there exists
t>t_{1}
such that the boundaries
\partial E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}
,
\partial E^{t+\varepsilon,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t+\varepsilon)}
, with the top point lying on the increasing wing of
C^{1}_{(t_{1},h_{1})}
, intersect each other outside of
E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
or never, but still we have
E^{t,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}\subset E^{t+\varepsilon,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{1}(t+\varepsilon)}\setminus E^{0,v_{2}}_{\hat{h}_{0}}
.

A.4.
Proof of Lemma
5.18

Suppose
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
with
s\leq s^{\prime}
and
h,h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
for which
U^{s}_{h}\cap U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset
and
\exists h_{1}\geq 0
such that
\partial^{*}U^{s}_{h}\cap\partial^{*}U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}=\{(0,h_{1})\}
. Observe that the area of the region
U^{s}_{h}\cup U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}
(see Figure
5.5
) is

(23)

\displaystyle|U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}\cup U^{s}_{h}|

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}|U^{s}_{h}|+|U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}\setminus U^{s}_{h}|,&\text{ if }s<s^{\prime}<0,\\
|U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}|+|U^{s}_{h}|-|U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}\cap U^{s}_{h}|,&\text{ if }s<0<s^{\prime},\\
|U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}|+|U^{s}_{h}\setminus U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}|,&\text{ if }0<s<s^{\prime}.\end{cases}

In the first case
s<s^{\prime}<0
, we have

\displaystyle|U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}\setminus U^{s}_{h}|

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{h_{1}}\int^{s^{\prime}+\left(h^{\prime 2}-y^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}_{s+\left(h^{2}-y^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}y{}

\displaystyle=h_{1}(s^{\prime}-s)+\int_{0}^{h_{1}}\left[\left(h^{\prime 2}-y^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\left(h^{2}-y^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\,{\rm d}y{}

\displaystyle=h_{1}(s^{\prime}-s)+{\frac{1}{2}}\left[h_{1}\left(h^{\prime 2}-h_{1}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+h^{\prime 2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h^{\prime})\right]-{\frac{1}{2}}\left[h_{1}\left(h^{2}-h_{1}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+h^{2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h)\right]{}

\displaystyle=h_{1}(s^{\prime}-s)+{\frac{1}{2}}h_{1}(|s^{\prime}|-|s|)+{\frac{1}{2}}\left[h^{\prime 2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h^{\prime})-h^{2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h)\right]{}

(24)

\displaystyle={\frac{1}{2}}h_{1}(s^{\prime}-s)+{\frac{1}{2}}F_{1}(h_{1},h,h^{\prime}),

where,

(25)

F_{1}(h_{1},h,h^{\prime}):=h^{\prime 2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h^{\prime})-h^{2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h).

In the second case
s<0<s^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle|U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}\cap U^{s}_{h}|

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{h_{1}}\int^{s+\left(h^{2}-y^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}_{s^{\prime}-\left(h^{\prime 2}-y^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}y{}

\displaystyle=-h_{1}(s^{\prime}-s)+\int_{0}^{h_{1}}\left[\left(h^{\prime 2}-y^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(h^{2}-y^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\,{\rm d}y{}

\displaystyle=-h_{1}(s^{\prime}-s)+{\frac{1}{2}}\left[h_{1}\left(h^{\prime 2}-h_{1}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+h^{\prime 2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h^{\prime})\right]+{\frac{1}{2}}\left[h_{1}\left(h^{2}-h_{1}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+h^{2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h)\right]{}

\displaystyle=-h_{1}(s^{\prime}-s)+{\frac{1}{2}}h_{1}(|s^{\prime}|+|s|)+{\frac{1}{2}}\left[h^{\prime 2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h^{\prime})+h^{2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h)\right]{}

(26)

\displaystyle=-{\frac{1}{2}}h_{1}(s^{\prime}-s)+{\frac{1}{2}}F_{2}(h_{1},h,h^{\prime}),

where,

(27)

F_{2}(h_{1},h,h^{\prime}):=h^{\prime 2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h^{\prime})+h^{2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h).

In the last case
0<s<s^{\prime}
, similarly we have

\displaystyle|U^{s}_{h}\setminus U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}|

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{h_{1}}\int^{s^{\prime}-\left(h^{\prime 2}-y^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}_{s-\left(h^{2}-y^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}y{}

\displaystyle=h_{1}(s^{\prime}-s)+\int_{0}^{h_{1}}\left[\left(h^{2}-y^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\left(h^{\prime 2}-y^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\,{\rm d}y{}

\displaystyle=h_{1}(s^{\prime}-s)+{\frac{1}{2}}\left[h_{1}\left(h^{2}-h_{1}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+h^{2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h)\right]-{\frac{1}{2}}\left[h_{1}\left(h^{\prime 2}-h_{1}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+h^{\prime 2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h^{\prime})\right]{}

\displaystyle=h_{1}(s^{\prime}-s)+{\frac{1}{2}}h_{1}(|s|-|s^{\prime}|)+{\frac{1}{2}}\left[h^{2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h)-h^{\prime 2}\arcsin(h_{1}/h^{\prime})\right]{}

(28)

\displaystyle={\frac{1}{2}}h_{1}(s^{\prime}-s)-{\frac{1}{2}}F_{1}(h_{1},h,h^{\prime}),

where the function
F_{1}
is defined in (
25
). Considering all the cases for
s,s^{\prime}
, we get the result by combining the expressions for
|U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}\setminus U^{s}_{h}|
,
|U^{s}_{h}\cap U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}|
and
|U^{s}_{h}\setminus U^{s^{\prime}}_{h^{\prime}}|
respectively from (
24
), (
26
) and (
28
) to (
23
). ∎

A.5.
Area of the regions
E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}\cup E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}

The area of union of half ellipses are computed using elementary geometry in the following Lemma. We compute the area of the region
E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}\cup E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}
for general speed
v
, also as preparation of the multi-speed case. For any speed
v
, two half-ellipses
E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}},E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}
are given by the equations

(29)

\displaystyle v^{2}(t-s_{1})^{2}+h^{2}=\ell_{1}^{2}\text{ and }v^{2}(t-s_{2})^{2}+h^{2}=\ell_{2}^{2}.

Let the intersection point in
\mathbb{H}^{+}
, of the two ellipses in (
29
) be
(t(v),h(v))
, which is given by

(30)

t(v):=\frac{1}{2(s_{2}-s_{1})}\left[(\ell_{1}^{2}-\ell_{2}^{2})/v^{2}-s_{1}^{2}+s_{2}^{2}\right]\text{ and }h(v):=\left(\ell_{1}^{2}-v^{2}(t(v)-s_{1})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Lemma A.4
.

Let
v>0
and
(s_{1},\ell_{1}),(s_{2},\ell_{2})
be two points such that
s_{1}<s_{2}
. Suppose the ellipses in (
29
) intersects at
(t(v),h(v))
. Then the area of the region
E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}\cup E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}
is given by

(31)

\displaystyle|E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}\cup E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}|

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2v}\left[\pi\ell_{1}^{2}+vh(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})+F_{1}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2})\right],&\text{ if }s_{1}<s_{2}<t(v),\\
\frac{1}{2v}\left[\pi\ell_{2}^{2}+\pi\ell_{1}^{2}+vh(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})-F_{2}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2})\right],&\text{ if }s_{1}<t(v)<s_{2},\\
\frac{1}{2v}\left[\pi\ell_{2}^{2}+vh(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})-F_{1}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2})\right],&\text{ if }t(v)<s_{1}<s_{2},\\
\end{cases}

where
t(v),h(v)
as in (
30
) and the functions
F_{1},F_{2}
are as in (
25
) and (
27
).

Proof.

t
\bullet
(t(v),h(v))
(s_{1},\ell_{1})
(s_{2},\ell_{2})
E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}
E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}

t
\bullet
(t(v),h(v))
(s_{1},\ell_{1})
(s_{2},\ell_{2})
E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}
E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}

t
\bullet
(t(v),h(v))
(s_{2},\ell_{2})
(s_{1},\ell_{1})
E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}
E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}

Figure A.8
.
Given
(s_{1},\ell_{1})
and
(s_{2},\ell_{2})
, the area of the union of two half-ellipses should be different for different speed and depending on
t(v)<s_{1}<s_{2}
or
s_{1}<t(v)<s_{2}
or
s_{1}<s_{2}<t(v)
.

The area of the region
E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}\cup E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}
is given by

(32)

\displaystyle|E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}\cup E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}|

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}|E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}|+|E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}\setminus E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}|,&\text{ if }s_{1}<s_{2}<t(v),\\
|E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}|+|E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}|-|E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}\cap E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}|,&\text{ if }s_{1}<t(v)<s_{2},\\
|E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}|+|E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}\setminus E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}|,&\text{ if }t(v)<s_{1}<s_{2}.\end{cases}

In the first case
s_{1}<s_{2}<t(v)
, we have

\displaystyle|E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}\setminus E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}|

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{h(v)}\int^{s_{2}+\frac{1}{v}\left(\ell_{2}^{2}-h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}_{s_{1}+\frac{1}{v}\left(\ell_{1}^{2}-h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,{\rm d}t\,{\rm d}h{}

\displaystyle=h(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})+\frac{1}{v}\int_{0}^{h(v)}\left(\left(\ell_{2}^{2}-h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\left(\ell_{1}^{2}-h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\,{\rm d}h{}

\displaystyle=h(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})+\frac{1}{2v}\left[h(v)\left(\ell_{2}^{2}-h(v)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-h(v)\left(\ell_{1}^{2}-h(v)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]{}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2v}\left[\ell_{2}^{2}\arcsin{(h(v)/\ell_{2})}-\ell_{1}^{2}\arcsin{(h(v)/\ell_{1})}\right]{}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref{eq:v_ellipses}}}{{=}}h(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})+\frac{1}{2v}\left[vh(v)|t(v)-s_{2}|-vh(v)|t(v)-s_{1}|+F_{1}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2})\right]{}

\displaystyle=h(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})-{\frac{1}{2}}h(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})+\frac{1}{2v}F_{1}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2}){}

(33)

\displaystyle={\frac{1}{2}}h(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})+\frac{1}{2v}F_{1}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2}),

where
F_{1}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2})
is defined in (
25
).
Let us now consider the second case, namely
s_{1}<t(v)<s_{2}
. Then
|E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}\cup E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}|=|E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}|+|E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}|-|E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}\cap E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}|
, where the area of the common region is

\displaystyle|E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}\cap E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}|

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{h(v)}\int_{s_{2}-\frac{1}{v}\left(\ell_{2}^{2}-h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{s_{1}+\frac{1}{v}\left(\ell_{1}^{2}-h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,{\rm d}t\,{\rm d}h{}

\displaystyle=h(v)(s_{1}-s_{2})+\frac{1}{2v}\left[h(v)\left(\ell_{1}^{2}-h(v)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+h(v)\left(\ell_{2}^{2}-h(v)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]{}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2v}\left[\ell_{1}^{2}\arcsin{(h(v)/\ell_{1})}+\ell_{2}^{2}\arcsin{(h(v)/\ell_{2})}\right]{}

\displaystyle=h(v)(s_{1}-s_{2})+\frac{1}{2v}\left[h(v)\left(\ell_{2}^{2}-h(v)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+h(v)\left(\ell_{1}^{2}-h(v)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]+\frac{1}{2v}F_{2}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2}){}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq:v_ellipses})}}{{=}}h(v)(s_{1}-s_{2})+{\frac{1}{2}}h(v)\left[|t(v)-s_{2}|+|t(v)-s_{1}|\right]+\frac{1}{2v}F_{2}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2}){}

(34)

\displaystyle=-{\frac{1}{2}}h(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})+\frac{1}{2v}F_{2}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2}),

since
|t(v)-s_{2}|+|t(v)-s_{1}|=s_{2}-s_{1}
, since
s_{1}\leq t(v)\leq s_{2}
, from the second picture of Figure
A.8
. Above
F_{2}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2})
is as defined in (
27
).
In the last case
t(v)<s_{1}<s_{2}
. Then we have
|E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}\cup E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}|=|E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}|+|E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}\setminus E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}|
. Thus

\displaystyle|E^{s_{1},v}_{\ell_{1}}\setminus E^{s_{2},v}_{\ell_{2}}|

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{h(v)}\int_{s_{1}-\frac{1}{v}\left(\ell_{1}^{2}-h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{s_{2}-\frac{1}{v}\left(\ell_{2}^{2}-h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,{\rm d}t\,{\rm d}h{}

\displaystyle=h(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})+\frac{1}{v}\int_{0}^{h(v)}\left(\left(\ell_{1}^{2}-h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\left(\ell_{2}^{2}-h^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\,{\rm d}h{}

\displaystyle=h(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})+\frac{1}{2v}\left[h(v)\left(\ell_{1}^{2}-h(v)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-h(v)\left(\ell_{2}^{2}-h(v)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]{}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2v}\left[\ell_{1}^{2}\arcsin{(h(v)/\ell_{1})}-\ell_{2}^{2}\arcsin{(h(v)/\ell_{2})}\right]{}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref{eq:v_ellipses}}}{{=}}h(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})+\frac{1}{2v}\left[vh(v)|t(v)-s_{1}|-vh(v)|t(v)-s_{2}|-F_{1}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2})\right]{}

\displaystyle=h(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})+{\frac{1}{2}}h(v)(s_{1}-s_{2})-\frac{1}{2v}F_{1}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2}){}

(35)

\displaystyle={\frac{1}{2}}h(v)(s_{2}-s_{1})-\frac{1}{2v}F_{1}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2}),

since
|t(v)-s_{1}|-|t(v)-s_{2}|=s_{1}-s_{2}
, as
t(v)<s_{1}<s_{2}
, see the first picture of Figure
A.8
. Above
F_{1}(h(v),\ell_{1},\ell_{2})
is as defined in (
25
). Thus we have the result.
∎
